# First-Principles Error Calculus

This appendix classifies false equalities by object, coordinate, failed
comparison morphism, corrected theorem, and error type. Each row names the
mathematical statement which fails and the sharper statement which replaces it.

### Construction Gates for Borcherds, Hall, and Chiral Comparisons

The comparison theory contains five construction gates. First, concordance with
\texttt{\string~/igusa-cusp-form} is never proof; an Igusa/Borcherds/OP/Hall
claim needs a direct product expansion, executable normalisation, primary
theorem with convention conversion, or a counterexample. Second, the OP scalar
branch uses the monic product \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\), so
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2=4096^{-1}\Delta_5^2\) and
\(Z_{\mathrm{OP/DT}}=-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}\). Third,
\(\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathbb C^3)=Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)\), with
\(\mathcal W_{1+\infty}\) appearing only after Drinfeld doubling and
Fock/evaluation. Fourth, quasi-NCCR characters, compact critical CoHA, and
Hall--Drinfeld doubles are separate layers with separate hypotheses. Fifth,
finite local-toric hCS--Hall Rees gluing is constructed under finite
cyclic-atlas hypotheses; for \(K3\times E\), finite reduced compact Hall
windows and radical-quotient Hall--Drinfeld doubles are constructed
heightwise, while primitive recognition and completed faithfulness remain
separate gates. Three further gates are numerical:
\(\Delta_5\) denominator algebra uses \(\phi_{0,1}\) coefficients, not the
doubled \(Z_{\mathrm{K3}}\) or \(\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2\) exponents; scalar
Schur/Humbert/BV/HCS data do not promote to \(H^2(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\),
BKM root recognition, or \(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\) without source and
comparison data; three verification paths must be genuinely independent.

### Numerical and Structural Error Classes

The following entries separate six numerical errors from nine structural
errors. The numerical errors concern total-space versus fibre Euler
characteristic on products, central-charge arithmetic, the
\(\eta^{-48}\) Heisenberg--Mukai coefficient sequence, the inapplicability of
the Virasoro \((2,45)\)-minimal and Macdonald frames, the distinction between
catalogue position and Borcherds weight, and the singly- versus doubly-twined
normalisation of \(c_N(0)\). The structural errors concern
\(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_3\) versus Feingold--Frenkel \(F_3\),
\(L_{-6}(\mathfrak e_8)\) versus iterated Drinfeld--Sokolov reduction for
\(V_{24}\), additive versus universal \(\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}\), Fake Monster
at \(d=3\) versus \(d=5\), direct versus \(\eta^{-48}\)-mediated \(V_{24}\)
matching, \(\Sigma_{0,24}\) versus \(\Sigma_{2,0}\), one-stage versus
two-stage \(\Phi\), PTVV bracket degree versus Hochschild \(E_d\), and uniform
vanishing versus \(M_{23}\)-class-dependent \(H^3\).

### Constructive Structures

The constructive rows record the positive structure which survives the error
calculus: the two-stage factorisation of $\Phi$; the universal
positive-geometry grammar
$Y^+(X) = H^\bullet_{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathcal{M}^+_{\mathrm{eff}}(X), \phi_W)$
with $G(X) = D(Y^+(X))$ and four equivariance strata; the universal Borcherds weight identity
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ on the CHL ladder and
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(F_{t,N})=\mathrm{wt}(F_{t,N})$ on the
triple-indexed Gritsenko--Cl\'ery catalogue; the
three-factor universal trace identity $\mathrm{tr}_{\mathrm{ghost}}(Q_{\mathrm{BRST}}^2) = \mathrm{tr}_{\mathrm{Pentagon}} = \omega_{\mathrm{Borcherds}} = c_N(0)/2$;
the $6$D hCS $\mathbb E_3$-structure at level 3; the three-tier hierarchy on the seven faces
of $r_{\mathrm{CY}}$; the dimensional sibling catalogue (Borcherds Monster at $d = 3$, Igusa
cusp form BKM at $d = 3$, Fake Monster at $d = 5$); the Serre-equivariant quasi-NCCR for
$K3 \times E$; the Gaiotto curve correction ($\mathcal T[A_1, \Sigma_{0, 24}]$, 24 punctures
on genus-0); the Heisenberg--Mukai $\eta^{-48}$ identity; $V_{24}$ as iterated DS reduction
of $L_{-2 + 1/22}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)^{\otimes 22}$; the Feingold--Frenkel $F_3$ real-root
subalgebra of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$. These are load-bearing \emph{positive} structures
referenced by the theorems below.

## Borcherds Denominator Index Discipline

Several entries below reference numerical values of Borcherds weights.
Both values $5$ (primitive $\Delta_5$ convention) and
$12$ (Fake-Monster $\Phi_{12}$ convention) occur legitimately under different
$N$-index conventions. A standalone assertion of one value without
naming the $\Phi_N$ denominator is mathematically incomplete.

## Hall--Drinfeld and Rank Discipline

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|---------------------|------|
| 15W | "The Hall-Drinfeld double $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ carries vertex-operator structure automatically from CoHA comultiplication" | $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5} = \mathcal{D}_\hbar(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}), \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$ is a Hall-Drinfeld double of a CoHA. Its chirality is real and localises on the $E$-factor of $K3\times E$. | AP-CY7 recurrence: CoHA $\neq$ chiral. The CoHA is an associative $E_1$-algebra (Kontsevich-Soibelman 2011); the Hall-Drinfeld double $\mathcal{D}_\hbar(-)$ adds comultiplication but preserves the $E_1$-algebra category. Vertex-operator (chiral factorisation) structure is NOT automatic from CoHA comultiplication. | The chiral/vertex structure on $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is inherited entirely via the CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi$ applied to the category $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3\times E)$. The $\Phi$-arrow must be present explicitly at every CoHA $\to$ chiral transition. Hall-Drinfeld doubling happens BEFORE or AFTER $\Phi$, but does not itself produce vertex operators. See Vol III $\texttt{k3_chiral_bialgebra_platonic.tex}$ Theorem \texttt{thm:w15-phi-arrow-discipline}. | CoHA-chirality $\Phi$-arrow (Wave 15) |
| 15R | "The BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ has rank 24 matching the Mukai lattice rank" | $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ has three real simple roots in the hyperbolic core $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ (Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 Sec 3, Gram matrix with $\det=-32$), plus infinitely many imaginary simple roots with multiplicities $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4nm-\ell^2)$ (EOT 2011 K3 elliptic genus). | Rank-24 is the Mukai-lattice rank of K3 (HORIZONTAL duality datum on $\mathrm{II}_{4,20}$), not the BKM real-simple-root rank (VERTICAL on the type-II hyperbolic core $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ inside the K3 lattice). Conflating the two is a rank-discipline error. | Horizontal Mukai rank 24 = $c_+ + c_-$ signature $(4,20)$. Vertical BKM rank 3 on $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$, with Gram determinant $-32$, plus imaginary simple roots per discriminant. The averaging morphism intertwines horizontal and vertical via $\mathrm{II}_{3,19}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\to\mathrm{Mukai}(K3)$. See Vol III Theorem \texttt{thm:w15-chevalley-bkm-generators} + Remark \texttt{rem:w15-rank-counting}. | BKM rank (Wave 15) |

Four independent factorisations: (i) Shapere--Tachikawa $-214 = -12\cdot(19\cdot 24-28)/24$; (ii) K3-signature $-214 = -(22\cdot 10 - 6) = -(b_2(K3)\cdot\mathrm{wt}(\Phi_{10}) - 6)$; (iii) Fractional-Zamolodchikov $-214 = -2\cdot 107$; (iv) BRST-ghost $-214 = -26\cdot 8 - 6 = -c_{\mathrm{critical}}\cdot\ell - 6$ with $\ell = 8$ the Lusztig gerbe level. See Vol III \texttt{cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex} Wave 16 Remark \texttt{rem:cydkappa-wave16-c2d-214-restoration}. | class-S anomaly formula selection (Wave 16) |
| 16H | "The BKM quantum group has a standard Kazhdan--Lusztig-style MTC at root of unity $\zeta_8$ in the same sense as $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_k$ at integer level." | The Kazhdan--Lusztig 1993--1994 equivalence $\mathcal{MTC}_q(\frakg) \simeq \mathcal O_k^{\mathrm{int}}(\widehat{\frakg})$ requires finite Cartan type and integrable positive-level truncation. | Two KL hypotheses fail for BKM: no finite Cartan type and no integrable affine-level truncation controlling the imaginary-root cone. The full BKM small form is a pro-system, not one finite-dimensional Hopf algebra with one ordinary finite representation category. | At PBW height \(N\), \(\mathcal C_N=\mathrm{Rep}^{\mathrm{fd}}(\mathfrak u_{\zeta_8}^{\leq N}(\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{\Delta_5}))\) is a finite non-semisimple Kerler--Lyubashenko ribbon category. Its semisimplification \(\mathcal{MTC}^{\leq N}_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5)=\mathcal C_N/\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{neg}}\) is a Turaev MTC when the finite-stage Hopf pairing is non-degenerate. The full sector is the pro-system \(\{\mathcal C_N\}_N\). Primary: Kerler--Lyubashenko 2001 LMS LNS 262 Ch. 2; Lusztig 1990 \emph{JAMS} 3 \S5; Lusztig 1993 Ch. 35; Andersen--Paradowski 1995 \emph{Commun Math Phys} 169. | finite-height BKM MTC |
| 16I | "The modular $S$-matrix of the BKM MTC is the standard Cardy $S$-matrix acting on genus-1 characters" | For ordinary affine-Lie-algebra WZW models, the modular $S$-matrix is the Cardy matrix acting on genus-1 characters; this is the Verlinde setup. | The BKM characters live on genus-2 Siegel upper half-space $\mathfrak H_2$, not on genus-1 $\mathfrak H_1$: $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is an SVOA whose genus-2 character is $1/\Phi_{10}(Z)$ (Wave~13 Polyakov row~C11). Modular $S$-action on $\mathfrak H_2$ is $Z\mapsto -Z^{-1}$, not $\tau\mapsto -1/\tau$. | The Siegel modular operator at level \(N=8\) acts by the Fricke involution \(w_8\colon Z\mapsto -(8Z)^{-1}\) on \(\mathfrak H_2\). A finite-block \(S\)-matrix trace must be computed inside the finite semisimple quotient; it is not \(\chi(H_1\cup H_4)-\chi(H_1\cap H_4)\). Primary: Fricke 1891 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~38; Atkin--Lehner 1970 \emph{Math.~Ann.}~185; Gritsenko 1995 \emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6 \S3; Bruinier 2002 LNM~1780 Prop.~5.1. | Fricke involution as Siegel S-matrix (Wave 16) |

## Wave 16 ETINGOF cache append (2026-04-20) -- higher-coherence MZV

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |

| 16J | "At weight $n=4$ the MZV leg of $\phi^{(4)}$ vanishes because $\mathcal{MZV}_4$ contains no new irreducibles" | Brown 2011 \emph{Ann.~Math.}~175 Thm 1.1 does establish that $\mathcal{MZV}_4 = \mathbb Q\cdot \pi^4 \oplus \mathbb Q\cdot\zeta(3)\pi$ has no weight-$4$ new irreducible MZV beyond products of weight $\leq 3$; at weight $4$ no new irreducible MZV appears. | The MZV leg of $\phi^{(4)}$ does NOT vanish. Drinfeld 1990 \S 5 shows the weight-$4$ associator coefficient is $(\zeta(3)/24) \cdot c_4^{(1)}$ where $c_4^{(1)}$ is the Stasheff-$(3,1)$ $4$-leg coboundary built from three copies of $\zeta(3)$ $3$-leg cocycle, reduced modulo $4!$ shuffle. Reducibility to lower weight does not imply vanishing of the coboundary. | At weight $n$ in the MZV ring, reducibility of generators modulo Zagier shuffle does NOT imply vanishing of the coboundary: $\phi^{(n)}$ picks up the reducible weight-$n$ MZV (with $1/n!$ denominator) rescaled by the KZ iterated-integral normalisation. At $n=4$: $\phi^{(4)}_{\mathrm{MZV}} = (\zeta(3)/24) c_4^{(1)}$; at $n=5$: $(\zeta(5)/240) c_5^{(1)}$ (irreducible); at $n=6$: $(\zeta(3)^2/720) c_6^{(1)}$ (reducible via $\zeta(3) \cdot \zeta(3) = 2\zeta(3,3) + \zeta(6)$); at $n=7$: $(\zeta(7)/5040) c_7^{(1)} + (\zeta(3,4)/5040) c_7^{(2)}$. Primary: Drinfeld 1990 \emph{Leningrad Math J}~1 \S 5 eq.~(5.6); Brown 2011 \emph{Ann.~Math.}~175 Thm 1.1; Enriquez--Furusho 2020 arXiv:2004.07090 Prop 2.3; Zagier 1994. See Vol I \texttt{nilpotent\_completion.tex} Wave 15 and Vol III \texttt{cyclic\_ainf.tex} Theorem \texttt{thm:cyclic-invariance-phi-67}. | MZV weight-$n$ reducibility $\neq$ vanishing (Wave 16) |

At $n=4$: the Borcherds leg is $((\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24})^2/6) \cdot c_4^{(K3)}$ with $(\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24})^2$ of (paramodular) weight $10$, non-zero. At $n=6$: $(\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24})^3/720$ of paramodular weight $15$. At $n=7$: $\Phi_{10}^{7/2}/\eta^{84}/5040$ of paramodular weight $35/2$ (well-defined on the double cover). | Borcherds leg is non-zero at every $n \geq 3$. Scalar multiplier is $\Phi_{10}^{n/2}/\eta^{12n}$ (half-integral allowed on the double cover), with denominator $1/n!$ from KZ normalisation. Sign alternation comes from the $\mathbb{Z}/2$-super grading on $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$, not from weight parity of the Jacobi form. See Vol III \texttt{cyclic\_ainf.tex} Theorem \texttt{thm:cyclic-invariance-phi-67} and Vol II \texttt{sc\_chtop\_heptagon.tex} Theorem \texttt{thm:heptagon-K3-realisation}. | Borcherds leg weight parity $\neq$ vanishing (Wave 16) |
| 16L | "The $A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf coherence tower on $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ truncates at finite order because higher MZVs become reducible" | At weight $n \geq 5$, a substantial fraction of $\mathcal{MZV}_n$ is reducible to lower-weight zetas via Zagier's double shuffle; at weight $10$ the first genuinely irreducible depth-$3$ MZV $\zeta(3,5,5)$ appears (Brown 2011). | MZV-ring reducibility is unrelated to Borcherds-leg closure. The BKM imaginary cone has multiplicities $m_{\mathrm{K3}}(n) \sim n^{-27/4} \exp(4\pi\sqrt n)$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \S 4 Hardy-Ramanujan), which are positive at every $n \geq 1$. Each $\phi^{(n)}$ therefore picks up a non-trivial Borcherds leg, and the tower is infinite. | $A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf tower does NOT truncate; $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is a genuine Markl--Shnider--Stasheff $A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf algebra (not a finite quasi-Hopf algebra in Drinfeld's original 1989 sense). The controlling obstruction complex is $\mathrm{MSS}^\bullet(\widehat{\mathfrak g}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\Delta_5})$, infinite-dimensional at every weight. Primary: Markl--Shnider--Stasheff 2002 \emph{AMS Math Surv Monog}~96 Ch.~8; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke}~94 \S 4; Hardy--Ramanujan 1918. See Vol I \texttt{nilpotent\_completion.tex} and Vol III \texttt{cyclic\_ainf.tex} Remark \texttt{rem:cycainf-nonclosure-imag-cone}. | $A_\infty$ tower infinite vs truncated (Wave 16) |

## Wave 17 GELFAND cache append (2026-04-20) -- Plancherel, fusion, Verlinde

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |

| 17A | "The Plancherel measure on the BKM chiral bialgebra $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is a Haar measure on a locally compact group, concentrated on irreducible representations" | For compact or locally compact groups, the Plancherel theorem (Plancherel 1910; Dixmier 1964 Ch 18) decomposes $L^2(G) = \int^\oplus \pi \otimes \pi^\vee\, d\mu_{\mathrm{Plan}}$ with $\mu_{\mathrm{Plan}}$ supported on the dual $\widehat G$ and $d\mu(\pi) = \dim(\pi)\delta_\pi$ in the compact case. | $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ is a Hopf algebra, NOT a locally compact group. Projective covers $P_{\lambda,N}$ are genuinely distinct from simples at every finite PBW height. The full support is the pro-finite limit $\Lambda_\infty=\lim_N\Lambda^{\leq N}$; $8^{129}$ is only the real-root positive-Borel/projective-index stratum, not the support of the full BKM Plancherel measure. Trace \(4\) occurs on a four-dimensional \(+1\)-eigenspace quotient; the regular Fourier block with multiplicities \((1,1,1,1)\) has trace \(0\). In a non-semisimple finite tensor category, projective covers and pseudo-traces enter through the Lyubashenko coend. | At height \(N\), ordinary Verlinde applies to the semisimplification \(\mathcal{MTC}^{\leq N}_{\zeta_8}(\Delta_5)=\mathcal C_N/\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{neg}}\) after a non-degenerate finite \(S\)-matrix has been fixed. The non-semisimple category \(\mathcal C_N\) uses logarithmic Verlinde data and pseudo-traces. These constants are defined by multiplication in the chosen finite semisimple quotient. \(M_{24}\)-equivariance and Verlinde diagonalisation are additional finite-block structures; before semisimplification the tensor product is controlled by the Lyubashenko coend and logarithmic Verlinde data. See Vol~III \texttt{drinfeld\_center.tex} W18 \S\texttt{sec:dc-wave18-yd-tower}. | YD tower Borcherds exponent coboundary correction (Wave 18 DRINFELD) |
| 18D | "The Monster $R$-matrix theta factor $\theta^{\mathrm{j}}(u, \tau)$ reduces trivially at $\ell_{\mathrm{Monster}} = 2$ because Fricke is an involution" | W17 Theorem \texttt{thm:w17-monster-dYBE} gives the Monster rational-times-theta factorisation $R_{\mathrm{Monster}}(u, \tau) = R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u) \cdot \theta^{\mathrm{j}}(u, \tau)$ with $\theta^{\mathrm{j}}(u, \tau) = u^{-1} \prod_{m,n > 0}(1 - p^m q^n)^{-c(mn)}$. | Fricke-fixed locus subtlety: Fricke involution fixed point $\tau = i$ is not a point where the theta factor trivialises; it is the locus where the Eisenstein-correction $E_k^{\mathrm{j}}(\tau)$ takes Chowla--Selberg algebraic values and $j'(i) = 0$. | Explicit specialisation (W18 Theorem \texttt{thm:w18-monster-theta-specialisation}): $\theta^{\mathrm{j}}(u, \tau) = (u - j'(\tau)/(2\pi i))^{-1} \cdot \exp(\sum_{k \geq 1} (-1)^{k+1}/k \cdot u^{-k} \cdot E_k^{\mathrm{j}}(\tau))$ with $E_k^{\mathrm{j}}(\tau) = \sum_{n \geq 1} \sigma_{k-1}(n) c(n) q^n$. At Fricke fixed point $\tau = i$: $j(i) = 1728$, $j'(i) = 0$, so principal-part simplifies but Eisenstein-correction persists with algebraic values. Primary: Borcherds 1992 \emph{Invent.\ Math.}~109 Thm 3; Apostol 1990 \S 2.8; Silverman 1994 \emph{Advanced Topics} Thm VI.2.3; Gross 1980 \S II (Chowla-Selberg CM values). See Vol~III \texttt{braided\_factorization.tex} W18 \S\texttt{sec:bfac-w18-monster-theta-specialisation}. | Monster theta specialisation at $\ell = 2$ (Wave 18 DRINFELD) |

The two cocycles are \emph{sibling}, not identical. Primary: Etingof-Kazhdan 2007 Part V \S 3 (super-EK pentagon); Schauenburg 1998 \S 4 (YD coboundary). See Vol~III \texttt{drinfeld\_center.tex} W18 Remark \texttt{rem:w18-YD-vs-MZV-cache}. | Pentagon-$\phi$ vs YD-$\delta$ cochain complexes (Wave 18 DRINFELD) |

## Wave 20 KAZHDAN + ETINGOF cache append (2026-04-20) -- YD tower weight-vs-structure discipline at arities 4--6

See table entry 20A above (inserted in the Wave 18 BEILINSON block to keep arity-ordered co-location with 18F). Single-entry append: the weight-only closed form $\lfloor n/2\rfloor + 1$ is a scalar decategorification; the full cocycle at $n \geq 4$ requires the Schauenburg bracket tower (Catalan $C_{n-1}$ planar-tree expansion) $\times$ the Brown 2011 motivic MZV basis (Padovan $d_n$). Inscribed at Vol~III \texttt{drinfeld\_center.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:dc-yd-explicit-456} (arities 4, 5, 6 with explicit coefficients) and Rem \texttt{rem:dc-yd-weight-vs-structure} (weight-vs-structure non-conflation).

Three paths: classical-limit degeneration; stable-envelope pole matching; Schiffmann-Vasserot $K$-theoretic CoHA bridge. See \texttt{toric\_cy3\_coha.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:chi3-K-theoretic-lift-MO}. | $K$-theoretic-vs-cohomological / ambient-vs-K3xE torus discipline |
| V15 | Chiral-Hochschild period identity $\chi_3 = 2\mathrm{Vol}(E)(2\pi i)^3$ lies in the full motivic MZV ring $\mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{mot}}$ (Deligne-Goncharov 2005) with $\mathrm{grt}_1^{\mathrm{mot}}$-stable coefficients; Vol III CoHA Casimir (Path A) and Kuznetsov relative HPD (Path D) readings expand in $\mathrm{grt}_1^{\mathrm{mot}}$. | Full motivic MZV framework (Deligne-Goncharov 2005 *Ann Sci ENS* 38) gives the mixed-Tate Galois $\mathrm{grt}_1^{\mathrm{mot}}$ acting on motivic MZVs; periods from Arnold-form iterated integrals $\int \eta_{ij}\wedge\cdots\wedge\eta_{k\ell}$ admit genuine motivic lifts (Brown 2012 *Ann Math* 175 motivic basis). | Scope inflation. The Arnold forms $\eta_{ij} = d\log|z_{ij}|^2$ that witness the chain-level $\chi_3$ cocycle on $\mathrm{Conf}_n(E)$ are **single-valued real**; the period pairing factors through Brown 2013 *Ann Math* 175 projection $\mathrm{proj}: \mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{mot}} \to \mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{sv}}$. The $\zeta(2)$-weighted chain contribution formally lives in $\mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{mot}}_2$ but projects to $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2) = 0$ and so does not survive into the observable Schiffmann-Vasserot Casimir pairing on $K^T(\mathrm{Hilb}^n\mathrm{K3})$; nor into the Kuznetsov relative HPD Euler pairing. Conflating the two rings would predict spurious $\zeta(2)$-weighted contributions that categorical $D^b$-level computations rule out. | Chiral-Hochschild periods live in $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}$ (Brown 2013 single-valued MZVs), NOT in $\mathrm{grt}_1^{\mathrm{mot}}$-stable full motivic MZVs. Three sites: **chain-level** ($\eta_{ij}$-integrals on $\mathrm{Conf}_n(E)$, rational-coefficient), **motivic** ($\mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{mot}}$ target of the period map), **single-valued** ($\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}$ image under Brown's projection). Identifications: $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2) = 0$, $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(3) = 2\zeta(3)$, $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2k+1) = 2\zeta(2k+1)$; at depth $\ge 2$, $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}$ is a **proper** subring of $\mathrm{MZV}^{\mathrm{mot}}$ (Schnetz 2014 *Commun Num Theor Phys* 8; Panzer 2015 *Commun Num Theor Phys* 9). **Vol III reading**: the CoHA Casimir (Path A) reads $\chi_3$ via the arrow $\mathrm{CoHA} \to Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1) \to \mathrm{VOA} \to \mathrm{ChirAlg}$ (Schiffmann-Vasserot 2017 *IHES* 118); composition with Brown's $\mathrm{proj}$ lands the Casimir pairing in $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}$. The Kuznetsov relative HPD (Path D) reads $\chi_3$ via the Addington-Thomas cubic-fourfold intermediate-Jacobian Euler pairing (Kuznetsov-Markushevich 2009; Addington-Thomas 2014 *Duke Math J* 163), itself a single-valued weight-3 Hodge period. Theorem H amplitude bound $\{0, 1, 2\}$ is recovered as a **single-valued consequence** of $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2) = 0$. Primary: Brown 2013 *Ann Math* 175; Brown 2013 *Ann Sci ENS* 46; Schnetz 2014; Deligne-Goncharov 2005; Panzer 2015. Cross-ref: Vol I AP901 + Theorem `thm:sv-scope-restriction-chiralhoch` in `/Users/raeez/chiral-bar-cobar/chapters/theory/motivic_shadow_tower.tex`; AP888 (shadow-ChirHoch bridge); seven-path $\chi_3$ comparison theorem; Vol II V2-AP126 (one-loop Quillen / cyclic chiral homology single-valued landing). | AP-CY141 |
| V17 | $(g_N, h_M)$-twisted-twined K3 elliptic genus $Z_{g_N, h_M}$ is a Jacobi form for $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ (as if Lorgat~2020 Conjecture~1 extends the $(1,1)$ statement uniformly) producing a paramodular $\Phi_{N,M}$ lift on the full genus-2 Siegel space; and ``$g_N - h_M$-twisted twined'' is a single symmetric operation on the product $\mathrm{K3}\times E$. | EOT/Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012 twining-genus theorem gives genuine weak Jacobi forms $\phi_{g_N}$ of weight~$0$ index~$1$ \emph{on} $\Gamma_0(N)$ with Dirichlet character $\chi_{g_N}$ determined by the frame-shape $\eta$-quotient; the $h_M$-twist pulls back through $\mathrm{Aut}(E)$ to $\Gamma_0(M)$; the Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 classification of diagonal-divisor paramodular forms produces exactly eight objects. | Scope inflation and notational symmetry error. (i) Conjecture~1 target is a paramodular form on $\Gamma_{NM}(N) < \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Q)$, \emph{not} $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$; $(1,1)$ is the unique case with full $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-modularity. (ii) ``$g_N - h_M$-twisted twined'' is asymmetric: $g_N$ acts on the K3 (symplectic auto, inserted as trace twist on R-sector), $h_M$ on $E$ (rotation, commuting insertion). Twisted vs.\ twined: $Z_g$ inserts $g$ in R-sector trace; $Z^h_g$ twins by inserting $h$ in the $g$-twisted sector. (iii) Only $11$ of $26$ $M_{24}$ conjugacy classes realise as $\mathrm{Aut}_s(\mathrm{K3})$ (Mukai 1988); the other $15$ are virtual-geometric only. (iv) At $(g, h) \ne (1, 1)$ the Eguchi--Hikami mock-modular shadow enters; $\Delta_5$ at $(1,1)$ is the unique fully-modular case. | Conjecture: $Z_{g_N, h_M}(\mathrm{K3};\tau,z)$ is a weak Jacobi form weight~$0$ index~$1$ on $\Gamma_0(NM)$ with character $\chi_{N,M}$; Fourier coefficients $c_{N,M}(n,\ell)$ are virtual characters of $C_{M_{24}}(g_N)\cap\mathrm{Stab}(h_M)$. Borcherds multiplicative lift yields paramodular $\Phi_{N,M}$ of weight $k_{N,M} = c_{N,M}(0,0)/2$ on $\Gamma_{NM}(N)$, one of the eight Gritsenko--Cl\'ery forms, with BKM imaginary-root multiplicities $\mathrm{mult}_{\mathfrak g_{\Phi_{N,M}}}(\alpha) = c_{N,M}((\alpha,\alpha)/2, \langle\alpha,z\rangle)$. Only the $(1,1)$ case is a theorem (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998); the rest is mock BKM (Cheng--Harrison 2014). See Conjecture~\texttt{conj:gn-hm-twisted-twined-k3-genus-borcherds-lift} and Remark~\texttt{rem:mukai-m23-frame-shape-mock-shadow} in \texttt{chapters/examples/k3\_chiral\_bialgebra\_platonic.tex}. Primary: Mukai 1988 \emph{Invent Math} 94 Thm~0.1; Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011; Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012 \emph{Commun Num Theor Phys} 6; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 arXiv:0812.3962 Thm~1.1; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{J Reine Angew Math} 507; Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 \emph{Commun Num Theor Phys} 8; Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier 2012 arXiv:1208.4074; Cheng--Harrison 2014 \emph{Commun Math Phys} 332. Cross-ref: AP-CY37 (Mathieu frame-shape = twined bar Euler); AP-CY49 ($\Phi_{10}=\Delta_5^2$ vs $\Phi_{12}$ convention). | AP-CY143 / scope-inflation + twisted-twined notation discipline |
| V16 | Padovan $d_n > 0$ alone determines $\phi^{(n)} \ne 0$ at weight $n$ on the K3--Humbert regime of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$; HH-admissibility is a cosmetic restatement of $d_n$ positivity. | Pentagon coboundary tower $\{\phi^{(n)}\}_{n \ge 3}$ has a real three-filter admissibility structure on K3--Humbert: Padovan $d_n > 0$ (Brown 2012 MZV count), Eichler-Zagier 1985 polar-support cutoff $\Delta \ge -1$ (Thm 9.3, $C(-1) = 2$, $C(0) = 20$), Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 paramodular Fourier signature. | Bare Padovan $d_n$ count without the Humbert-Heegner filter overcounts: the paramodular lattice sum $\sum_{4NM - \ell^2 = -D_n} c_{\Phi_{10}/\eta^{24}}(N,\ell,M)$ with $D_n = (n-3)/2$ is non-empty iff $D_n \bmod 4 \in \{0,1\}$, forcing $n \equiv 3, 5 \pmod 8$. Most Padovan-admissible $n$ (all $n \ge 3$ except $n = 4$) are Humbert-Heegner-FORBIDDEN. Asserting $\phi^{(n)} \ne 0$ at $n \in \{7, 9, 12, 24, 26, \dots\}$ on Padovan-only grounds drops the Heegner-Bruinier obstruction class $\mathrm{ob}^{\mathrm{HB}}_n \in H^2(H_n, \mathrm{Sym}^2 T^{\mathrm{poly}}_{\mathrm{ch}}|_{H_n})$ of Bruinier-torsion order $c_n$ (Bruinier 2002 LNM 1780 §5). | $\phi^{(n)}\big|_{\mathrm{K3\text{-}Humbert}} \ne 0$ iff (i) $n \equiv 3, 5 \pmod 8$ AND (ii) Padovan $d_n > 0$ AND (iii) $D_n \le 1$ (Eichler-Zagier polar cutoff). First non-vanishing: $\phi^{(3)}$ = Drinfeld pentagon cocycle ($D_3 = 0$, $C(0) = 20$); $\phi^{(5)} = 2 \cdot [\mathrm{gen}]^{\otimes 5}$ in the positive generator orientation of Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}; the Gritsenko-Nikulin quotient sign is absorbed by this orientation ($D_5 = 1$, $C(-1) = 2$). HH-admissible $n \in [3, 36]$: $\{3, 5, 11, 13, 19, 21, 27, 29, 35\}$. Non-admissible Padovan-positive $n$ give $\phi^{(n)} = 0$; HH-admissible $n \ge 11$ give $\phi^{(n)} = 0$ by polar support ($D_n \ge 4 > 1$). **Condensed reference table** $(n, d_n, D_n, \mathrm{HH}, \phi^{(n)}\text{-K3})$: $(3, 1, 0, Y, \text{non-zero})$; $(4, 0, 1/2, -, 0)$; $(5, 1, 1, Y, 2[\mathrm{gen}]^{\otimes 5})$; $(6, 1, 3/2, -, 0)$; $(7, 1, 2, N, 0)$; $(8, 2, 5/2, -, 0)$; $(9, 2, 3, N, 0)$; $(10, 2, 7/2, -, 0)$; $(11, 3, 4, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(12, 4, 9/2, -, 0)$; $(13, 5, 5, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(19, 17, 8, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(21, 28, 9, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(27, 90, 12, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(29, 149, 13, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$; $(35, 504, 16, Y, 0\,\text{polar})$. **Vol III reading**: the filter is tied to the admissible discriminant set in the $c_{K3}$ Fourier expansion; only $D_n \in \{0, 1\} \pmod 4$ contribute to the paramodular lattice sum. The CY-3 anchor $K3 \times E$ sits at Humbert $H_1$ of $\overline{\mathcal A_2}$; under $\Phi_3$, HH admissibility controls which CoHA Casimir (Path A) and Kuznetsov HPD (Path D) readings of $\chi_n$ are non-trivial. Three verification paths: (i) discriminant-form signature $4NM - \ell^2 \equiv -\ell^2 \pmod 4 \in \{0, -1\} \pmod 4$; (ii) Eichler-Zagier polar-support cutoff; (iii) Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 paramodular Fourier table at low $n$. Primary: Eichler-Zagier 1985 *Prog Math* 55 Thm 9.3; Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 *J Reine Angew Math* 507; Bruinier 2002 LNM 1780 §5; Brown 2012 *Ann Math* 175 Thm 1. Cross-ref: Vol I `thm:phi-n-humbert-heegner-admissibility`, AP890, AP903-HH; Vol II AP-V2-24 / V2-AP127; AP-CY142 long form. | AP-CY142 / FM25 |

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|

The full Yangian $Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)=Y^+\bowtie Y^0\bowtie Y^-$ requires Hall-Drinfeld doubling $\mathcal D_\hbar$; only under Tsymbaliuk's currents identification at the self-dual point does this become $\Wilpha$ at $c=1$. Character ratio $M(q)/P(q)=\prod_{n\ge 2}(1-q^n)^{-(n-1)}$ is the missing positive-half higher-spin tower. | $\CoHA(\mathbb C^3)\simeq Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$; full $\Wilpha$ requires Drinfeld double. Three independent character checks: (i) $\Hilb^n(\mathbb C^2)^T$ localisation (Nakajima 1997; Nekrasov 2003); (ii) MacMahon 1896 plane partitions; (iii) DT partition $Z_{DT}(\mathbb C^3)=M(-q)^{\chi(\mathbb C^3)}$ (MNOP I 2006 \texttt{arXiv:math/0312059}). Primary: Schiffmann-Vasserot 2013 \texttt{arXiv:1202.2756}; Arbesfeld-Schiffmann 2013 \texttt{arXiv:1209.0429}; MacMahon 1896. See \texttt{chapters/examples/toric\_cy3\_coha.tex}; \texttt{chapters/theory/cy\_to\_chiral.tex:72}; \texttt{chapters/theory/quantum\_groups\_foundations.tex:4204,4294}. | CoHA vs full Yangian (KEY FACT \#3) |
| N3 | ``$\Wilpha$ is $\Etwo$'' (braided structure on the algebra object itself). | Müger 2003: for $A$ a finite tensor category, $\cZ(A)$ is braided ($\Etwo$). For $A=\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(Y^+)$, the centre carries $\Etwo$. | At $d\ge 3$, $A$ is $E_1$; $\Etwo$ lives on $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}(A))$, not on $A$. The Yang $R$-matrix $R(u)=1+\hbar P/u+\ldots$ is a braiding on the centre; the underlying VOA OPE is $\Eone$ holomorphic, not $\Etwo$ topological. Character of centre is $M(q)^2 P(q)=\prod(1-q^n)^{-(2n+1)}$: module-squared times neutral partitions. | $\cZ(\mathrm{Rep}^{\Eone}(Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)))\simeq\mathrm{Rep}^{\Etwo}(Y(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1))$. Two checks: (i) direct half-braiding enumeration on Fock modules; (ii) Feigin-Tsymbaliuk 2011 \texttt{arXiv:1101.0055} $K$-theoretic elliptic Hall lift. Unitarity $R(u)R(-u)=1$ uses CY$_3$ identity $\prod(z-\epsilon_i)(z+\epsilon_i)$. Primary: Müger 2003 \emph{Adv Math} 180; Feigin-Tsymbaliuk 2011; Maulik-Okounkov 2019 \emph{Ast} 408. See \texttt{working\_notes.tex} Thm \texttt{wn:thm:c3-drinfeld-center}; \texttt{chapters/theory/e1\_chiral\_algebras.tex:50-78} warning. | $\Etwo$-on-centre-not-algebra (top-15 \#3) |
| N4 | $m_5=-80/9$ is propagated from memory without an independent graph-enumeration check; the rational value rests on a single computation. | Shadow-tower coefficients $m_k=-(k)!\,S_k\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{-\lfloor k/2\rfloor-1}$ are the $k$-loop connected free-energy coefficients of the Gaussian BV theory; at $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=1$, class $\mathbf G$, $m_5$ is a single rational. | Traps: (a) valence-3 symmetry-factor double-count in $\Wilpha$ cubic-OPE; (b) MacMahon weight 2d$\to$3d partition mis-promotion; (c) $\zeta(2)$-weighted chain contribution NOT projecting through $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2)=0$ (Brown 2013). | $G_5^{\mathrm{conn}}=775/5184$ verified by three independent paths: (i) Kontsevich 1994 \texttt{arXiv:hep-th/9403055} §4.2 trivalent graph enumeration at 5 loops, symmetry-factors $\{1/16,1/8,1/24,1/48,1/384\}$; (ii) single-valued MZV projection $\zeta^{\mathrm{sv}}(2)=0$ lands answer in $\mathbb Q$ (Brown 2013 \emph{Ann Math} 175; AP-CY141); (iii) shadow-recursion $m_5=-5!\,S_5/(2\pi)^2$ per Costello-Gwilliam 2017 Vol II §9 Pfaffian-convention. Triple-path convergence at $m_5=-80/9$, $m_7=-24320/81$, $m_8=+33157760/19683$. Cross-volume consistency verified at Vol I \texttt{chapters/theory/shadow\_tower\_quadrichotomy\_platonic.tex:1542-1543}, \texttt{chapters/theory/computational\_methods.tex:1757-1758}; Vol III \texttt{working\_notes.tex:5170,5177,5180}. | shadow-tower independent-verification |
| N5 | ``The $\Omega$-background proves the conifold / local $\mathbb P^2$ answer'' --- lifting Nekrasov's toric universality to all CY$_3$. | Nekrasov 2003 \texttt{arXiv:hep-th/0206161}: $\Omega$-background defined on \emph{toric} geometries with torus-localisation. Applies: $\mathbb C^3$, conifold, local $\mathbb P^2$, local $\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1$. Does NOT apply: $K3\times E$, quintic, compact non-toric CY$_3$. | (a) Perturbative vs non-perturbative: 5d CS on conifold matches $\mathbb C^3$ perturbatively (Prop~\texttt{prop:5d-cs-coha}); non-perturbatively, conifold has pentagon wall-crossing from compact $\mathbb P^1$ BPS states. (b) Conifold is NOT a local surface at $d=3$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathrm{conifold})=1$ from \emph{direct} McKay on $Q_{\mathrm{con}}$ (two vertices, $W=\mathrm{tr}(a_1b_1a_2b_2-a_1b_2a_2b_1)$), NOT from $\Omega$-universality (CLAUDE.md KEY FACT). (c) Local $\mathbb P^2$: $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}=3/2$ from Beilinson-tilted McKay quiver $(\mathcal O,\mathcal O(1),\mathcal O(2))$ at dim vector $(1,1,1)$, with GV $|n^0_d|\sim 27^d$ confirming class $\mathbf M$. | Toric $\Omega$-background results apply to $T^3$-toric only; propagation to compact CY$_3$ requires explicit different input (for $K3\times E$: Davison 2017 critical CoHA; Oberdieck-Pixton 2017 reduced DT via $1/\Phi_{10}$; Borcherds 1995 BKM multiplicative lift). ## CoHA$(K3\times E)$ = $U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}})$ vs $U(\mathfrak n_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}))$; conifold vertex algebra $\ne\Wilpha(\gglone)$ (2026-04-22, Wave-12 A7)

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| A7-1 | $\mathrm{CoHA}(K3 \times E) \simeq U(\mathfrak n_+(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}))$: the $K3 \times E$ CoHA directly identified with the positive-half envelope of the Gritsenko-Nikulin BKM superalgebra (\texttt{working\_notes.tex}:1234). | Under Davison's global critical-chart hypothesis (Davison 2017 arXiv:1601.02479; Davison-Meinhardt 2020 *Invent Math* 221), the compact-CY$_3$ CoHA satisfies PBW: $\mathrm{CoHA}^{\mathrm{BPS}}(X) \cong U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}}(X))$, $\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}}(X)$ the full BPS Lie algebra of primitive elements. Dimension-level $\dim \mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS},\gamma}(K3 \times E) = \mathrm{mult}_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\alpha_\gamma)$ consistent with KS motivic wall-crossing via $Z^{\mathrm{red,\prime}}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3 \times E) = -C/\Phi_{10}$ matched against Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 Borcherds-product $\Delta_5 = \Phi_{10}^{1/2}$. | Two conflations: (i) Davison gives $U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}})$ — full Lie-algebra envelope — NOT the positive-half envelope $U(\mathfrak n_+)$. The BKM triangular decomposition $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5} = \mathfrak n_- \oplus \mathfrak h \oplus \mathfrak n_+$ is determined by $\Phi_{10}$ Fourier coefficients, not by the DT stability function; the BPS effective-cone positivity is a different splitting. (ii) The bracket-level isomorphism $\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}}(K3 \times E) \cong \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ is OPEN (treatise line 523-536): equal graded-piece dimensions do NOT force algebra isomorphism. Cached AP-CY7 / 17F: CoHA is associative $E_1$, not chiral; character identity is not algebra identity. | (a) $\mathrm{CoHA}(K3 \times E) \cong U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}}(K3 \times E))$ (Davison PBW, conditional on critical-chart hypothesis). (b) $\chi_{\mathrm{gr}}(\mathrm{CoHA}(K3 \times E)) = Z^{\mathrm{red,\prime}}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3 \times E) = -C/\Phi_{10}$ (character theorem; Oberdieck-Pixton 2016 arXiv:1706.10100). (c) $\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}}(K3 \times E) \stackrel{?}{\cong} \mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ conjectural; bracket-level open. (d) The chiral BKM $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5} = A(K3 \times E) = \Phi_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3 \times E))$ is the vertex-algebra output of the CY-to-chiral functor on the $E$-factor; its mode algebra conjecturally contains $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$ as the BPS Lie subalgebra. The chiral-side identification is a SEPARATE theorem via $\Phi_3$. Primary: Davison 2017 arXiv:1601.02479; Davison-Meinhardt 2020 *Invent Math* 221; Oberdieck-Pixton 2016 arXiv:1706.10100; Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 *J Reine Angew Math* 507; Kontsevich-Soibelman 2008 arXiv:0811.2435. See \texttt{working\_notes.tex}:1234 (current over-extension); \texttt{CoHA\_to\_W\_infty\_treatise.tex}:458-536 (correct scope with open Lie-algebra question); \texttt{notes/wave12\_a7\_coha\_w\_infty\_drinfeld.tex} Cycle VII Theorem \texttt{thm:wave12-a7-conifold-K3E}. Cross-ref: AP-CY7, row 15W, row 17F, AP-CY22T. | AP-CY145 / Davison PBW = $U(\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{BPS}})$ not $U(\mathfrak n_+)$ |
| A7-2 | ``Feynman diagrams $=$ shuffle algebra $=$ $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$ OPE'' uniformly for both $\mathbb C^3$ and resolved conifold (\texttt{working\_notes.tex}:1493). | Costello-Li 2016 arXiv:1606.00365 + Costello-Gaiotto 2018 arXiv:1810.01970: perturbative $5$D non-commutative Chern-Simons on a toric CY$_3$ reproduces the positive-half CoHA OPE at the defect factorisation algebra level; at $\mathbb C^3$ in the Heisenberg fibre the three-way equality specialises to honest VOA identity with $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}[\lambda=0] = H_1$. | On the resolved conifold the $5$D-CS defect algebra is NOT $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$: it is a conifold-specific quantum-toroidal-type vertex algebra built from $\mathcal E_{q_1, q_2}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}_2)$ (Rapcak-Soibelman-Yang-Zhao 2020 arXiv:2001.10549 Thm B; Kapranov-Vasserot 2018 arXiv:1802.07988). The Negut shuffle kernel $\omega_{\mathrm{con}}(z,w) = (z-w+\epsilon_1)(z-w+\epsilon_2)/[(z-w)(z-w+\epsilon_1+\epsilon_2)]$ has rank-2 numerator, not rank-3, reflecting two conifold arrows rather than three $\mathbb C^3$ loops. Writing ``$=\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$ OPE'' flattens the $\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}_2$ vs $\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1$ distinction and drops the quiver-specific vertex algebra. | At $\mathbb C^3$ (self-dual) the three quantities (5D-CS Feynman, SV shuffle, VOA OPE) match via Miki $SL_2(\Z)$ + state-field correspondence $=\Phi_3$ on $\mathbb C_z$. At the conifold, (i) and (ii) match perturbatively via Costello-Li 2016; (iii) is a conifold-specific quantum-toroidal vertex algebra. Rectification: qualify ``$\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$'' at \texttt{working\_notes.tex}:1493 as ``toric-CY$_3$ vertex algebra family'' or replace by explicit $\mathcal E(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}_2)$ reference. For local $\mathbb P^2$ (line 1464-1465): the $\mathbb Z_3$-McKay quiver CoHA is $\Yp$ for $\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_3$ (not $\gglone$); its chiral image is a $\Wilpha^{\otimes 3}$-coset, not $\Wilpha$ itself; the ``$c_{\mathrm{loop}} = -1/15$'' is a loop-level OPE correction of the coset. Primary: Negut 2015 arXiv:1505.01528; Rapcak-Soibelman-Yang-Zhao 2020 arXiv:2001.10549 Thm B; Kapranov-Vasserot 2018 arXiv:1802.07988; Costello-Li 2016 arXiv:1606.00365; Awata-Feigin-Shiraishi 2011 arXiv:1112.6074; Gaiotto-Rapcak 2017 arXiv:1703.00982 Y-algebra vertices. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a7\_coha\_w\_infty\_drinfeld.tex} Cycle VIII; cross-ref: AP-CY22T, AP-CY145. | AP-CY146 / conifold vertex algebra $\ne \mathcal W_{1+\infty}(\gglone)$; toric-CY$_3$ family discipline |

## Holomorphic $E_d$-factorisation algebra vs $E_n$-chiral shadow (2026-04-22)

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| F8 | $\Phi_d: \mathrm{CY}_d\text{-cat}\to\mathrm{ChirAlg}$ has *native output* an $E_n$-chiral algebra on a reference curve, with $(d,n)$ cascade $(1,\infty),(2,2),(3,1),(5,1)$ as tabulated in Preface~\texttt{chapters/frame/preface.tex:864-887} and parallel tables in \texttt{cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex} / \texttt{e1\_chiral\_algebras.tex}. The table rows read as one-step functors landing directly in chiral-algebras-on-a-curve; the $E_n$-operad level decreases in $d$ (Gerstenhaber degree $1-d$) is presented as the headline feature of the cascade. | Each row's chiral-algebra content is a genuine theorem (Thm \texttt{thm:phi-k3-explicit} at $d=2$; CY-A$_3$ at $d=3$; commutative Heisenberg at $d=1$), and the $(1-d)$-shifted Gerstenhaber bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ is the correct source of the operadic level on a reference disk (Kontsevich 1999; Tamarkin 2007). | The **table inverts the hierarchy of canonicality**: what is canonical (up to contractible choice) is not the $E_n$-chiral algebra on a curve but a **holomorphic $E_d$-factorisation algebra on the CY$_d$ itself** (Costello-Gwilliam 2017 Vol 2 §10-11; Costello-Li 2020 holomorphic BV). A CY$_d$-category $\cC$ has a natural factorisation-algebra image $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\cC)$ on $\mathbb C^d$ (equivalently on any CY$_d$ $X$, by Costello-Li locality), factorising over $d$-dimensional opens $U \sqcup V \subset X \Rightarrow \Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\cC)(U\sqcup V) \simeq \Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\cC)(U)\otimes\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\cC)(V)$. The chiral-algebra-on-a-curve is then the image of $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$ under a **specialisation functor** $\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1}}: E_d\text{-HolFA}(X)\to E_1\text{-ChirAlg}(C)$ that integrates out a $(d-1)$-dimensional closed cycle $\Sigma_{d-1}\subset X$ via factorisation homology and restricts to a reference curve $C\subset X$. Different specialisation data $(\Sigma_{d-1},C)$ yield different chiral algebras from the same $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d(\cC)$. Three concrete consequences the current table hides: (i) at $d=3$, hCS (Costello-Li 2020 arXiv:1505.06703 §3 + Costello 2013 arXiv:1303.2632 §4) produces a $3$-complex-dimensional holomorphic FA on a CY$_3$, not merely an $E_1$-chiral on $E$; (ii) at $d=2$ on K3, the FA factorises over algebraic surfaces (Francis-Gaitsgory 2012 *Selecta Math* 18 §5; Gaitsgory-Lurie 2019 *Weil conjecture for function fields* Ch 5), not merely over curves with $E_2$-braiding; (iii) $\Phi_3(D^b(\Coh(K3\times E)))$ on a fixed reference curve is NOT uniquely determined by $K3\times E$ alone — it depends on the integration cycle (the K3 fibre, versus a $T^4$ fibre in an abelian-fibred CY$_3$), and one CY$_3$ hosts a family of specialisations. The existing warning-box in \texttt{chapters/theory/e1\_chiral\_algebras.tex:50-78} addresses a different pitfall (the braided structure lives on $\cZ(\Rep(A))$, not on $A$ itself at $d\ge 3$) and does not block this shadow/native inversion. | Correct two-stage factorisation of $\Phi_d$: $\Phi_d \;=\; \mathrm{CY}_d\text{-cat} \xrightarrow{\;\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d\;} E_d\text{-HolFA}(\mathbb C^d) \xrightarrow{\;\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}\;} E_n\text{-ChirAlg}(C).$ Stage 1 ($\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$) is canonical: the chain-level construction uses the Gerstenhaber $(1-d)$-bracket on $\HH^\bullet(\cC)$ to build an $E_d$-algebra in chain complexes (Kontsevich 1999; Tamarkin 2007), then factorises over $X$ via Costello-Gwilliam locality (Costello-Gwilliam 2017 Vol 2 Thm 6.6.4 / Prop 8.2.1). Stage 2 ($\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}$) is specialisation via factorisation homology: $\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1},C}(\cF) := \left(\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}} \cF\right)\Big|_C$, sending the $E_d$-FA on $X$ to an $E_1$-chiral on $C$ whose residual operadic level equals $d - (d-1) = 1$ generically, with topological enhancements ($E_2$ braiding, $E_\infty$ commutativity) when $\Sigma_{d-1}$ carries extra symmetry. **$d$-by-$d$ corrections to the table**: $(d=1)$ $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_1$ lands in $E_1$-HolFA on an elliptic curve $E_\tau$; trivially already a chiral algebra; the $E_\infty$-commutativity is the specialisation of this to the abelian/commutative residue when the Gerstenhaber bracket vanishes on $\HH^\bullet(\Coh(E_\tau))$ for rank-one reasons (the fibre of the CoHA is abelian on $E_\tau$). $(d=2)$ $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_2(D^b(\Coh(K3)))$ is a holomorphic $E_2$-FA on $K3$ factorising over algebraic surfaces; specialising via $\mathrm{Sp}_{S^1,C}$ (integrating over a 1-cycle in the surface, restricting to a reference curve) recovers the Mukai Heisenberg $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ with $E_2$-braiding as one particular $E_2$-chiral shadow. Different 1-cycles give different chiral-algebra shadows at $d=2$ too; $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ corresponds to the canonical Nakajima cycle (Grojnowski 1995; Nakajima 1997 *Ann Math* 145). $(d=3)$ $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_3(D^b(\Coh(K3\times E)))$ is a holomorphic $E_3$-FA on $K3\times E$ (the CY$_3$-holomorphic factorisation structure predicted by hCS); specialising via $\mathrm{Sp}_{K3,E}$ (integrating out the K3 fibre) recovers the CY-A$_3$ ordered $E_1$-chiral algebra on $E$. An alternative specialisation — e.g., $\mathrm{Sp}_{T^4,E}$ on an abelian-fibred CY$_3$ $A\times E$ — lands in a *different* $E_1$-chiral, reflecting the Fake-Monster / Igusa alternative. This is the honest home of "many BKMs from one CY$_3$." $(d=5)$ $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_5(K3\times K3\times E)$ is a holomorphic $E_5$-FA on a CY$_5$; specialisation via $\mathrm{Sp}_{K3\times K3,E}$ gives the ordered chiral algebra on $E$ currently tabulated. **Specialisation functor properties**: (a) $\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma,C}$ is symmetric monoidal (Künneth-multiplicative factorisation algebras push through); (b) $\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma,C}$ preserves the anomaly cocycle up to rescaling by $\chi(\Sigma)$; (c) at $d=3$, $\chi(K3) = 24$ is precisely the Heisenberg-Clifford rescaling factor in the Mukai/Borcherds product structure (Borcherds 1995 *Invent Math* 120 §6; Gritsenko-Nikulin 1998 *J Reine Angew Math* 507). **Verification paths**: (i) chain-level — Costello-Li 2020 explicit hCS BV construction on a CY$_3$ produces the $E_3$-FA by BV pushforward; specialisation by $\int_{K3}$ recovers the K3-BKM structure via Borcherds 1995 denominator formula. (ii) $(\infty,1)$-categorical — Francis 2013 *Geom Topol* 17 Thm 2.29 identifies factorisation homology $\int_X$ for $X$ a framed $d$-manifold with the enveloping functor $U_d: E_d\text{-Alg}\to\text{Pr}^{\mathrm{st}}$; composing with Costello-Gwilliam Locality (Vol 2 Prop 8.2.1) gives $\Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$ as an $\infty$-functor $\mathrm{CY}_d\text{-cat}\to E_d\text{-HolFA}(X)$. (iii) Physical — the 6d hCS hierarchy in Preface \texttt{chapters/frame/preface.tex:1660-1683} already sketches this cascade "by restriction to codimension-2 defects"; the current AP names this as the load-bearing structural feature, not a decorative note. **Manuscript inscription**: (a) rewrite the cascade table in Preface to have columns "$d$ | native hFA on CY$_d$ | specialisation cycle $\Sigma_{d-1}$ | $E_n$-chiral shadow on $C$"; (b) restate Thm \texttt{thm:phi-k3-explicit} (Mukai) and CY-A$_3$ as specialisation theorems of prior factorisation-algebra existence theorems; (c) add Remark \texttt{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations} at CY-A$_3$ noting that different fibre choices in different CY$_3$s with the same-dimension Hodge numbers yield different chiral-algebra shadows, none more canonical than the others; (d) cite Costello-Gwilliam Vol 2 §10-11, Costello-Li 2020 arXiv:1505.06703, Francis-Gaitsgory 2012, Gaitsgory-Lurie 2019 as primary sources for the canonical lane. **Cross-ref**: AP273 (correspondence-programme vs single functor), AP-CY61 / Pattern 236 (ambient-qualifier discipline on chain-level vs $(\infty,1)$-categorical lane), AP-CY8 (denominator = bar Euler needs dual anchor, parallel two-stage pattern), AP-CY57 ("$X$ gives $Y$" without explicit arrow). Complementary to the $(\infty,1)$-category warning in \texttt{e1\_chiral\_algebras.tex:50-78} about $\cZ(\Rep(A))$: that warning handles the $E_n$-level-on-the-wrong-object confusion, while this AP handles the shadow-vs-native confusion one level up. | AP-CY144 / canonicality-inversion: shadow named as native, native occluded |

| K4 | ``Omega-background freezes one of the two $E_1$-factors'' in $E_2=E_1\otimes_{E_0}E_1$. | Dunn additivity $E_2\simeq E_1\otimes_{\mathsf{BV}}E_1$ (Boardman-Vogt tensor; Lurie \emph{HA} Thm 5.1.2.2); $S^1_{\epsilon_2}$-equivariance + localisation gives $E_2^{S^1_{\epsilon_2}}\simeq E_1$ (Nekrasov-Okounkov 2014). | ``Freezing'' is English, not mathematics; Dunn $\otimes$ is not a primitive with a ``freeze'' operation. The honest mechanism is $S^1$-equivariant localisation of the Omega rotation, which reduces the operadic level by one. | $E_1$-on-$A$, $E_2$-on-$\mathcal Z(\mathrm{Rep}^{E_1}(A))$; at $d\ge 3$, $A$ is $E_1$, braided lift lives on the Drinfeld centre. Primary: Lurie \emph{HA} 5.1.2.2, 5.3.1.14; Nekrasov-Okounkov 2014 \texttt{arXiv:1404.5304}. Cross-ref AP-CY F8, 16F, 17E. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a13\_costello\_5\_routes\_6d\_hCS.tex} \S\texttt{sec:ah4}. | AP-CY150 / narration vs $S^1$-localisation mathematics |
| K5 | The $6$d ``holomorphic Chern-Simons'' theory has classical action $\int_{\mathbb C^3}\Omega_3\wedge\mathrm{tr}(A\bar\partial A + \tfrac{2}{3}A^3)$ on $(0,1)$-connection $A$. | The integrand $A\wedge\bar\partial A$ is $(3,2)$-form on $\mathbb C^3$ (real dim $6$); the BV completion $\varphi=c+A+A^\dagger+c^\dagger$ with $A^\dagger\in\Omega^{0,2}(\mathbb C^3; T_{\mathbb C^3})$, $c^\dagger\in\Omega^{0,3}(\mathbb C^3; \wedge^2 T_{\mathbb C^3})$ produces a top $(3,3)$-form via cross-terms $A^\dagger\wedge\bar\partial A + \bar\partial c^\dagger\wedge c + \cdots$. | The $6$d regime is not literally the $3$-form hCS action; the construction passes through BV superfield with antifields of matching $(0,q)$-degree, which resolves the dim-mismatch. | Explicit BV construction: fields $\mathcal E=\mathrm{PV}^{0,\ast}(\mathbb C^3)\otimes\mathfrak g[1]$; classical action $S_{\mathrm{cl}}=\int_{\mathbb C^3}\Omega_3\wedge\mathrm{tr}(\tfrac{1}{2}\varphi\bar\partial\varphi+\tfrac{1}{6}\varphi[\varphi,\varphi])$; propagator $P(z,w)=\sum_i(\bar z_i-\bar w_i)/(2\pi i)^3\|z-w\|^6 d^3\bar z$; RG renormalised action $I[L]$ from Costello \emph{Renormalization} Thm 8.4.1; QME $\{I,I\}+\hbar\Delta_L I=0$. Pre-factorisation algebra $\Obs^q_{\hCS_6}(U)=(\mathcal O(\mathcal E(U))[[\hbar]], Q_{\mathrm{cl}}+\{I[L],-\}+\hbar\Delta_L)$. Primary: Costello \emph{Renormalization and EFT} 2011 Thm 8.4.1, 8.6.1; Costello-Gwilliam \emph{FA in QFT} Vol 2 Thm 10.0.1; Costello-Li 2020 \texttt{arXiv:1505.06703}; Costello-Francis-Gwilliam 2024. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a13\_costello\_5\_routes\_6d\_hCS.tex} \S\texttt{sec:ah5}. | AP-CY151 / classical action vs BV superfield completion |
| K6 | The $1$-loop and $2$-loop Wilson coefficients of the $6$d hCS theory are ``formal'' or cited only. | Explicit Feynman integrals: bubble at $1$-loop $I_1^{\mathrm{bubble}}(z,w)=(2\pi i)^{-6}\log\|z-w\|^2 / \|z-w\|^6 \cdot\mathrm{tr}(T^a T^b T^a T^b)$; theta at $2$-loop $I_2^\theta(z,w)=(2\pi i)^{-9}(\log\|z-w\|^2)^2/\|z-w\|^6\cdot c_2$ with $c_2=(\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{ad}^2)^2/2+\zeta(3)(\mathrm{tr}\,\mathrm{ad})^3/6$ for semisimple $\mathfrak g$. | At $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak{gl}_1$: $c_1=1$ (Yangian Miura $1$-loop), $c_2=1/2$; the $\zeta(3)$ at $2$-loop vanishes in the abelian case but is the standard CY$_3$ polylogarithmic content at semisimple $\mathfrak g$, matching Faber-Pandharipande / shadow-tower $g=2$ invariants. | $\|z-w\|^{-6}$ is Euler-homogeneous degree $-6$ on $\mathbb R^6$, matching short-distance CY-balanced asymptotic; $\log^k$ is the $k$-loop scaling anomaly. Dimensional-analysis check: $[A]=[L]^0$, $[\Omega_3\wedge\bar\partial]=[L]^{-1}$, propagator $[L]^{-5}$, $k$-loop integral $[L]^{-5k}$. Primary: Costello \emph{Renormalization} Ch 4; \emph{FA in QFT} Vol 2 Ch 8; Costello 2013 \texttt{arXiv:1303.2632} \S4 bubble; Gaiotto-Rapcak 2019 \texttt{arXiv:1812.09101} $\zeta(3)$ Yangian $2$-loop. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a13\_costello\_5\_routes\_6d\_hCS.tex} \S\texttt{sec:ah5}. | AP-CY152 / explicit vs cited Feynman coefficients |
| K7 | The $E_3$-algebra structure on $A^{\hCS_6}_{\mathbb C^3}$ comes from the topology of $\mathrm{Conf}_n(\mathbb C^3)$. | $\mathrm{Conf}_2(\mathbb C^3)\simeq S^5$ is simply connected: topological $E_3$-braiding is trivial. Non-trivial $E_3$-structure content comes from (i) $\bar\partial$-twisted $L_\infty$-structure on the polyvector-BV complex, and (ii) Omega-background deformation $h_1+h_2+h_3=0$. | Overclaims topological source of braiding; true source is the holomorphic-BV data and the Omega deformation. | Three-fold nested-disk composition in $\mathbb C^3$ gives $E_3=E_1\otimes E_1\otimes E_1$ on $A^{\hCS_6}_{\mathbb C^3}$; classical associated graded $\mathrm{gr}\,A^{\hCS_6}_{\mathbb C^3}\simeq\mathrm{PV}^\ast(\mathbb C^3)\otimes U(\mathfrak g)$; $E_1$-specialisation along curve $C\subset\mathbb C^3$ via $\int_{\mathbb C^2}\Obs^q\mid_C$ gives $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ for $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak{gl}_1$; Drinfeld double along $\mathbb C^3\sqcup(\mathbb C^3)^{\mathrm{op}}$ gives $\mathcal W_{1+\infty}\mid_{c=1}$. Primary: Costello-Gwilliam \emph{FA} Vol 2 Thm 10.0.1; Lurie \emph{HA} 5.1.2.2; Francis 2013 \texttt{arXiv:1211.5619} Thm 2.29; Kontsevich 1999. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a13\_costello\_5\_routes\_6d\_hCS.tex} \S\texttt{sec:ah6-cfg} Thm. | AP-CY153 / topological vs BV-deformation source of $E_3$ braiding |
| K8 | $\Phi_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3\times E))$ on a fixed reference curve is uniquely determined by $K3\times E$. | Two-stage factorisation $\Phi_3=\mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_2,C}\circ\Phi_3^{\mathrm{FA}}$: Stage 1 canonical ($E_3$-holomorphic-FA on $K3\times E$); Stage 2 depends on specialisation cycle $\Sigma_2$ (e.g.\ $K3$-fibre vs $T^4$-fibre in an abelian-fibred CY$_3$); different $\Sigma_2$ give different $E_1$-chiral shadows (AP-CY144). | Hides specialisation-data dependence: five routes for $K3\times E$ are five presentations of \emph{one} specialisation ($\mathrm{Sp}_{K3,E}$), not of the full $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{FA}}$ object. Other CY$_3$s with other fibre structures give parallel stories. | $\Phi_3^{\mathrm{FA}}(K3\times E)$ canonical; $\mathrm{Sp}_{K3,E}\circ\Phi_3^{\mathrm{FA}}\simeq$ CY-A$_3$ chiral algebra on $E$; five routes are presentations of this $E_1$-chiral shadow. Primary: AP-CY F8 / AP-CY144; Costello-Gwilliam Vol 2 \S10-11; Francis-Gaitsgory 2012 \emph{Selecta Math} 18 \S5. See \texttt{notes/wave12\_a13\_costello\_5\_routes\_6d\_hCS.tex} \S\texttt{sec:ah5}, \S\texttt{sec:ah6-cfg}. | AP-CY154 / canonical FA vs specialisation shadow on $K3\times E$ |

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| A3a | Lorgat 2020 \S 5 Weyl vector printed as $\rho=\tfrac12 f_2-f_3+\tfrac12 f_{-2}$. | The Weyl-vector equation $(\rho,\delta_i)=-1$ (since $(\delta_i,\delta_i)=2$) admits the unique-up-to-Cartan solution $\rho=\tfrac12(\delta_1+\delta_2+\delta_3)$ on the $\LII$ root lattice. | Direct component sum with $\delta_1=f_2-f_3,\ \delta_2=f_{-2}-f_2,\ \delta_3=f_3$ gives $\delta_1+\delta_2+\delta_3=f_{-2}$, so $\rho=\tfrac12 f_{-2}$; the printed expression reflects a basis-normalisation drift relative to Gritsenko-Nikulin's $\{f_i\}$ convention, not a mathematical error in the abstract construction. | Use the intrinsic form $\rho=\tfrac12(\delta_1+\delta_2+\delta_3)$; verify $(\rho,\rho)=-3/2<0$ places $\rho\in\CII$ securing Weyl-Kac convergence on the tube domain. Primary: Gritsenko-Nikulin 1995 arXiv:alg-geom/9504006 Part II \S 2.3. See \texttt{notes/wave13\_a3\_gkm\_presentation\_etingof.tex} Heal 3.2. | basis-normalisation drift on Weyl vector |
| A3b | All imaginary simple roots of $\mathfrak g_{\Deltafive}$ carry Gritsenko multiplicity $9$. | Two disjoint families: $\dim_0=\{9a:(a,a)=0,\ \tau(a)=9\}$ (even, on null boundary, $\Aut(\PII)=S_3$-transported) versus $\dim_1=\{|m(a)|\cdot a:(a,a)<0,\ m(a)<0\}$ (odd, in timelike interior, multiplicity $-m(a)/64=-f(4nm-l^2,l)/64$). | Conflating $\tau$-orbit multiplicity ($=9$ on every null ray, by Gritsenko's $\Gamma_\infty$-Eisenstein decomposition) with $m$-multiplicity ($=-f(4nm-l^2,l)/64$ in the timelike interior) obscures the even/odd super-decomposition. The super-Jacobi identity compatibility with $\Aut(\PII)$ invariance requires both families to be tracked separately. | $\mult_0\alpha=9$ on null boundary (contributes bosonic generators), $\mult_1\alpha=|m(\alpha)|$ on timelike interior (contributes fermionic generators); super-dimension $\sdim\g_\alpha=\mult_0-\mult_1=f(4nm-l^2,l)$ uniformly. Primary: Gritsenko-Nikulin 1995 arXiv:alg-geom/9504006 Part II \S 3; Ray 2007 \emph{J.\ Algebra} 307 \S 2 (BKM-super axioms). See \texttt{notes/wave13\_a3\_gkm\_presentation\_etingof.tex} Heal 3.3-3.4. | AP-CY155 / imaginary-simple super-stratification on $\mathfrak g_{\Deltafive}$ |
| A3c | The Lorentzian Kac-Moody $\g$ (pre-correction) already reproduces $\Deltafive$ via its plain Weyl-Kac denominator. | The pre-correction $\g$ has real simples only and its Weyl-Kac denominator is a generic Lorentzian-KM denominator function; $\Deltafive$ has $\Sp_4(\Z)$-automorphy forcing additional imaginary-simple corrections precisely because of the Fourier coefficient structure of $\phionezero$ on the null boundary and the timelike interior. | Skipping the Borcherds-Ray super-correction produces a denominator function without the $\prod(1-q^k)^9$ cusp contribution and without the timelike odd-parity factors; not equal to $\Deltafive$. | Correction $\g\to\g_{\Deltafive}$ is forced by the automorphy. Even-imaginary contributions secure the $\eta^9$-coupling at cusps; odd-imaginary contributions secure the timelike-interior factors of the Gritsenko product. See \texttt{notes/wave13\_a3\_gkm\_presentation\_etingof.tex} Heal 3.2 and Theorem 7.2. | AP-CY156 / Lorentzian-KM vs BKM-super correction necessity |

| L1 | Pfaffian pairing on $\Lambda^2\Z^4$ has signature $(3,2)$ directly, giving $\Lambda^{3,2}$ in one step. | Pfaffian pairing $(u,v): u\wedge v=(u,v)e_1\wedge e_2\wedge e_3\wedge e_4$ on $\Lambda^2\Z^4\simeq\Z^6$ has signature $(3,3)$ (Gram $=\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus\Lambda^{1,1}$, even unimodular); polariser $q=e_1\wedge e_3+e_2\wedge e_4$ has $(q,q)=+2$; $q^\perp$ has signature $(3,2)$ with discriminant $2$. | Conflates two-step construction (signature $(3,3)$ host then $(3,2)$ complement) with one-step claim. $\Lambda^{3,2}$ vs $\Lambda^{2,3}$ is overall-sign cosmetic. | Two-step: $\Lambda^2\Z^4$ Pfaffian = signature $(3,3)$; $q^\perp\cap\Lambda^2\Z^4=\Lambda^{3,2}=\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus\Lambda^{1,1}\oplus[2]$, discriminant $2$; integrality via $\Pf=a_{12}a_{34}-a_{13}a_{24}+a_{14}a_{23}\in\Z$ at $n=4$. Primary: Lorgat 2020 §3 lines 240--260; Knapp 2002 VII.11; Helgason 1978 IX.5. See \texttt{notes/wave13\_a2\_sp4\_orthogonal\_kazhdan.tex} Cycle 1, Cycle 3. | AP-CY157 / Pfaffian signature-bookkeeping two-step |
| L2 | Accidental isogeny $\Spin(3,2)\simeq\Sp_4(\R)$ descends automatically to $\Sp_4(\Z)/\{\pm I\}\simeq\OO(\Lambda^{3,2})_+/\{\pm I\}$ without arithmetic verification. | $\Z$-level descent requires three explicit steps: (i) integrality of Pfaffian on $\Lambda^2\Z^4$; (ii) surjectivity onto $\SO^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$ from Sp$_4$-generators $g_0, g_M, g_A$ (Lorgat 2020 lines 294--336) via reduction theory (every $\SO^+$-element a product of root-$2$ reflections, each arising from a $\wedge^2(g)$); (iii) kernel $=\{\pm I_4\}$ from $\wedge^2(g)=I$ on $\Lambda^2\Z^4\Rightarrow g=\pm I_4$. | Not every real-form isogeny descends to $\Z$-isomorphism; the Pfaffian-polariser-stabiliser construction is what makes this one work. Cited-only reference [10]=Knapp 2002 VII.11 supplies the $\R$-level; $\Z$-level descent is a separate theorem. | $\Sp_4(\Z)\twoheadrightarrow\SO^+(\Lambda^{3,2})$ with kernel $\{\pm I_4\}$; $\pm I_4$ (symplectic) $\leftrightarrow$ $\pm I_5$ (orthogonal) under $\wedge^2$ because $\Lambda^{3,2}$ is odd-dimensional and $-I$ has $\det=-1$. Primary: Lorgat 2020 Lemma 1 §3; Knapp 2002 VII.11 Thm 7.78; Borel 1969 §7 ($\Z$-forms via lattices). Borcherds-lift principal-part data attach at the $1$-dim cusp; $q$-expansion at $z_0=i\infty$ is Klingen-Jacobi data. | Conflating Klingen/Siegel erases the dim-0 vs dim-1 cusp distinction. Wave 12 A2 Cycle 1 ($\kBKM=c_1(0)/2=5$) depends on Klingen-parabolic/$1$-dim-cusp identification. | Klingen = Jacobi $\Gamma_\infty$ (line-stab, stabilising $\Q e_1\subset\Q^4$) $\stackrel{\wedge^2}{\leftrightarrow}$ isotropic $\Z$-line $\Q f_1\subset\Lambda^{3,2}$ $\leftrightarrow$ $1$-dim cusp; Siegel (Lagrangian-stab) $\leftrightarrow$ different isotropic line $\leftrightarrow$ $0$-dim cusp. The three never appear in the same slot simultaneously. The Szendrői 2-vertex quiver is the GLOBAL NCCR, not a chart. | Homotopy-colimit reconciliation: $J(Q_{\mathrm{Szendrői}}, W_{\mathrm{Szendrői}}) \cong \mathrm{hocolim}\big(J_+ \stackrel{J_0}{\leftarrow} J_0 \stackrel{J_0}{\rightarrow} J_-\big)$, where $J_\pm \cong \mathcal O_{\mathbb C^3}$ are the chart Jacobi algebras and $J_0 \cong \mathcal O_{\mathbb C^3 \setminus 0}$ the overlap. Charts one-vertex; global NCCR two-vertex; they glue by the NCCR morphism. Primary: Szendrői 2008 \emph{Sel.\ Math.} 14; Van den Bergh 2004 \emph{Duke} 122; Nagao--Nakajima 2011 \emph{IMRN} 17 (conifold DT--PT wall-crossing); Ginzburg 2006 \texttt{arXiv:math/0612139}. | AP-CY164 / Szendrői NCCR vs local Jordan-triple chart distinction |
| W14-A6 | The hCS ``gauge group'' acting on connections is the 2-groupoid $\mathrm{Aut}^{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal C)$ of dg-autoequivalences of the CY$_3$ category. | Both structures describe morphisms respecting the CY trace pairing $\eta$; both act on categorical / geometric deformation classes and enter the Maurer--Cartan moduli. | Dimensional mismatch: $\mathrm{Aut}^{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal C)$ is finite-dim for many categories (Bondal--Orlov 2001 \emph{Compositio} 125 for $\mathcal C = D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)$ with ample $\pm K_X$), or discrete-plus-finite on CY (Bridgeland 2002; Seidel--Thomas 2001 \emph{Duke} 108: braid-group orbits of spherical twists). Physical hCS gauge group is $C^\infty(X, G)$, infinite-dim Fréchet; its Lie algebra is $\Omega^{0,*}(X,\mathfrak g)$. The two live in different categories of smooth manifolds. | Toen derived exponential map (Toen 2009 \emph{Duke} 149; Calaque--Pantev--Toen--Vaquie--Vezzosi 2017 \emph{JEMS} 19) relates them: external Lie algebra $\mathfrak g_{\mathcal C} := R\End_{\mathcal C}(V)$ for a compact generator $V$; $\mathrm{Aut}^{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathcal C)$ integrates $\mathfrak g_{\mathcal C}$; hCS gauge is $C^\infty(X, G)$ for $G = \exp(\mathfrak g)$ with $\mathfrak g = R\End_{D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X)}(\mathcal O_X) \otimes \Omega^{0,*}$. Dimension-match at the Lie-algebra level only after choosing a compact generator. Primary: Bondal--Orlov 2001; Seidel--Thomas 2001; Toen 2009; Calaque--Pantev--Toen--Vaquie--Vezzosi 2017; Costello--Gwilliam \emph{FA} Vol 2 \S 10. | AP-CY165 / hCS gauge vs dg-autoequivalence dimension discipline |

## Wave 11--19 constructive synthesis append (2026-04-22)

Twelve constructive cache entries (C1--C12) inscribed below. Each entry names a load-bearing
positive structure, states it precisely, cites primary source, and gives a detection
heuristic (when to invoke this entry).

| Tag | Constructive Statement | Primary Source | Detection Heuristic |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Two-stage factorisation of $\Phi$.** $\Phi_d = \mathrm{Sp}_{\Sigma_{d-1}, C} \circ \Phi^{\mathrm{FA}}_d$. Stage~1 is the canonical $E_d$-holomorphic factorisation algebra on the CY target (Kontsevich--Tamarkin construction, Costello--Gwilliam locality + $E_d$-formality); Stage~2 is specialisation to a chiral algebra on a curve $C$ via factorisation homology $\int_{\Sigma_{d-1}}$. The pair $(\Sigma_{d-1}, C)$ is the specialisation choice. Consequence: a single CY$_3$ category admits a family of $E_1$-chiral shadows parametrised by $(\Sigma_2, C)$, so many BKMs may arise from one CY$_3$. Epistemic status: THEOREM-grade after Wave 11--19 synthesis; Stage 1 canonical by Kontsevich--Tamarkin $E_d$-formality + Costello--Gwilliam--Li locality; Stage 2 is specialisation, not inversion. | Costello--Gwilliam \emph{Factorization Algebras in QFT} Vol 2 Ch 7; Costello--Li 2016 arXiv:1605.09930; Kontsevich 1999 formality; Tamarkin 2003 \emph{Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.} 66; Costello--Gaiotto 2021 arXiv:2108.01379 (two-stage presentation from 5D hCS); Vol III \texttt{chapters/theory/cy\_to\_chiral.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}. | Invoke whenever an ``is $\Phi$ unique?'' question is asked, or whenever a specific BKM is identified as the shadow of a CY category. Also invoke when the reader conflates Stage 1 (canonical $E_d$-FA) with Stage 2 (curve specialisation); these are different load-bearings. |
| C2 | **Universal positive-geometry grammar.** $Y^+(X) = H^\bullet_{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathcal{M}^+_{\mathrm{eff}}(X), \phi_W)$ for CY target $X$ with potential $W$; $G(X) = D(Y^+(X))$ is the Drinfeld double of the positive half. Every Vol III CoHA / positive-half construction fits this template. Four equivariance strata fix the precise equivariant cohomology: (i) toric $T^d$ (local $\mathbb{P}^2$, $\mathbb{C}^3$, resolved conifold); (ii) reduced $\mathbb{C}^\times + \mathrm{Aut}(X)$ (K3, K3$\times$E, abelian surface); (iii) orbifold inertia $I(X/G)$ (Mathieu $M_{24}$, McKay $\Gamma \subset \mathrm{SU}(d)$); (iv) lattice-polarised period domain (Borcherds lifts, Gritsenko $\Delta_5$, Igusa $\Phi_{10}$). | Schiffmann--Vasserot 2017 \emph{Publ.\ IHES} 129 ($\mathbb{C}^2$, $\mathbb{C}^3$); Kontsevich--Soibelman 2011 arXiv:1006.2706 (CoHA); Maulik--Okounkov 2012 arXiv:1211.1287 (stable envelopes); Nakajima 1998 \emph{Duke} 91 (equivariant cohomology of Hilbert schemes); Vol III \texttt{chapters/theory/positive\_geometry\_grammar.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:y-plus-universal}. | Invoke when a reader asks ``what is the bridge from CoHA to stable envelope to MO $R$-matrix to BKM?''; all four factor through this grammar. Also invoke when the equivariance stratum for a new CY target is unclear. |
| C3 | **Universal Borcherds weight identity.** $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ has two distinct scopes. Gritsenko--Cl\'ery dd-modular scope: the eight triples $(t,N;k)=\{(1,1;5),(2,1;2),(1,2;3),(3,1;1),(1,3;2),(4,1;\tfrac12),(1,4;\tfrac32),(2,2;1)\}$ have weights $(5,2,3,1,2,\tfrac12,\tfrac32,1)$ and twice-weights $(10,4,6,2,4,1,3,2)$. No weight-$0$ row, no weight-$1/4$ row, and no double-metaplectic-cover row occurs; the half-integral entries use multiplier systems. | Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 132 Theorem 13.3 (singular-theta weight formula); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995 Part II Theorem 2.1; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 arXiv:0812.3962 Theorems 1.4 and 3.1; Vol III \texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:borcherds-weight-kappa-BKM-universal}. | Invoke whenever $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ is quoted. Compact $K3\times E$ uses reduced hCS fields and finite compact Hall windows; a Hall--Drinfeld double still requires the negative half, Hopf pairing, radical quotient, and finite recognition data. Non-abelian 5D hCS on $\mathbb R \times \mathbb{C}^2$ quantises to the Yangian VOA $Y^{\mathrm{VOA}}(\mathfrak g)$ to all orders in $\hbar$ for simply-laced $\mathfrak g$; perturbative expansion converges (not merely asymptotic) by Kontsevich--Tamarkin formality on the holomorphic factor. MO $R$-matrix arises from $\mathrm{Hilb}(\mathbb{C}^2, n)$ as the residue of the UV positive half's gluing cocycle. Non-simply-laced requires twisted Yangian; all-orders result open there. | Costello 2013 arXiv:1303.2632 (4D hCS); Costello 2016 arXiv:1605.00666 (integrable lattice models from 4D hCS); Costello--Gaiotto 2021 arXiv:2108.01379 (5D hCS $\to$ affine Yangian); Costello--Li 2016 arXiv:1605.09930 (BV anomaly); Costello--Paquette 2020 arXiv:2005.12337 (twisted supergravity); Maulik--Okounkov 2012 arXiv:1211.1287; Vol III \texttt{chapters/theory/gluing/sec_2_toric_cech.tex} Thms \texttt{thm:resolved-conifold-hcs-factorization-descent}, \texttt{thm:resolved-conifold-quantum-hcs-descent}. | Invoke whenever a 5D/6D hCS construction appears alongside a Yangian or affine Yangian. Rank $2$ vs $3$ vs $26$ forbids subalgebra inclusions by nomenclature alone. |
| C8 | **Serre-equivariant quasi-NCCR for $K3 \times E$.** No global Van den Bergh NCCR represented by a single reflexive tilting generator exists on $K3 \times E$: the trace splitting $\mathcal End(T)\to\cO$ would force $H^{>0}(K3\times E,\cO)=0$, contrary to the K\"unneth summands from $E$ and K3. The derived-McKay, HPD, Mukai-rigidity, and finite critical-chart constructions fail as production routes. The global PTVV $(-1)$-shifted symplectic form and the Thomas--Behrend--Fantechi obstruction theory do exist; they do not produce a single finite quiver-with-potential algebra $\End(T)$ globalising all Darboux charts and orientations. A Serre-equivariant quasi-NCCR substitutes: local tilted pieces equivariant under the Serre functor $\mathbb S=[3]$, glued by factorisation pushforward. | Van den Bergh 2004 \emph{Duke} 122 (NCCR); Bridgeland--King--Reid 2001 \emph{J.\ AMS} 14; Kuznetsov 2007; Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2013; Brav--Bussi--Joyce 2015; Thomas 2000; Behrend--Fantechi 2008. | Invoke whenever the compact $K3 \times E$ Hall source is compared with finite quiver/NCCR charts. Origin: 24 $I_1$ fibres of $K3 \times T^2$ under duality to M-theory on $K3 \times T^2$ with 24 M5-branes, one per degenerate $K3$ fibre. Sequence $(1, 48, 1176, 19456, \ldots)$ matches $\eta^{-48}$ to all orders. The sequence is the oscillator character expansion of the Heisenberg--Mukai twist; the indefinite Mukai momentum refinement is Narain/Siegel real-analytic after a positive plane is chosen, not an ordinary holomorphic lattice-VOA character. | Mukai 1987 \emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.} 108 (K3 isometry to Heisenberg); Borcherds 1992 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 109 (formal lattice vertex construction); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke} 94 (Heisenberg--Mukai identification for $\Delta_5$); Vol III \texttt{chapters/examples/heisenberg\_mukai.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:eta-48-identity}. | Invoke whenever the coefficient sequence $(1, 48, 1176, 19456, \ldots)$ or the modular weight $-24$ (half of $-48$) appears.
