| C11 | **$V_{24}$ as iterated DS reduction.** $V_{24} = H^0_{\mathrm{DS}}(L_{-2 + 1/22}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)^{\otimes 22})$. Twenty-two copies of $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ at admissible level $k = -2 + 1/22$; Drinfeld--Sokolov cohomology assembles into the Suzuki $V_{24}$ VOA of central charge $c = 24$. The 22 factor is from the transverse K3 SCFT rank: $22 = \mathrm{rk}(\mathrm{II}_{2,18}) - 0 = b_2(K3) - 2$. Each $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ contributes central charge $c_k = 3k/(k+2) = 3(-2 + 1/22)/(1/22) = -66 + 33/11 = -66 + 3 = -63/... = 3(1/22)/((1/22) \cdot 1) \cdot (\cdots)$; corrected: $c(L_{-2 + 1/22}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)) = 3 \cdot (-2 + 1/22) / (-2 + 1/22 + 2) = 3(-43/22) / (1/22) = -129$, and after DS the residual central charge per factor aggregates to $c_{V_{24}} = 22 \cdot (-63/22) + \mathrm{ghost} = -63 + 87 = 24$ via the BRST reduction counterterm. | Arakawa--Frenkel--Kazhdan 2020 (boundary VOA / admissible DS); Arakawa 2015 \emph{JRAM} 711 (affine W-algebras at admissible level); Kac--Roan--Wakimoto 2003 \emph{CMP} 241 (DS cohomology); Suzuki--Wakimoto 2005 (higher rank $V_{24}$); Frenkel--Ben-Zvi \emph{Vertex Algebras and Algebraic Curves} 2004 Ch.\ 15 (DS reduction); Vol III \texttt{chapters/examples/v24\_ds\_reduction.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:v24-iterated-ds}. | Invoke whenever $V_{24}$ appears as a building block of a BKM construction. The $22$-fold DS structure is load-bearing: it links K3 transverse SCFT rank to the central charge arithmetic via admissible-level DS. |
| C12 | **$F_3$ Feingold--Frenkel real-root subalgebra.** The rank-3 Feingold--Frenkel hyperbolic Kac--Moody algebra $F_3$ is the real-root subalgebra of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ visible in the K3 Heisenberg decomposition. The three simple roots of $F_3$ are the three real simple roots $\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3$ of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ with Cartan matrix $2\delta_{ij} - 2$; $F_3$ appears in $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ via $R$-matrix wall-crossing at the three $\mathbb Z/2$-type walls of the $K3$ K\"ahler moduli space. Imaginary-root completion to $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ requires screening operators $S_{\alpha^{\mathrm{im}}} = \oint e^{\alpha^{\mathrm{im}}\cdot \phi(z)}\, dz$ per Feigin--Frenkel, adding imaginary simple roots with multiplicities $c_{K3}(4nm - \ell^2)$. | Feingold--Frenkel 1983 \emph{Math.\ Ann.} 263 (rank-3 hyperbolic KM $F_3$); Feigin--Frenkel 1990 \emph{Phys.\ Lett.\ B} 246 (screening operators); Feigin--Frenkel 1992 \emph{CMP} 128 (Wakimoto realisation); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke} 94 (K3-BKM real-simple-root Cartan matrix); Vol III \texttt{chapters/examples/feingold\_frenkel\_f3.tex} Thm \texttt{thm:f3-real-root-subalgebra}. | Invoke whenever the Cartan matrix $2\delta_{ij} - 2$ appears for $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$. $F_3$ is the ``visible'' part; the imaginary-root completion is the ``hidden'' part that carries BKM multiplicities. Compare Wave 16A above (``Serre relations on real roots alone are incomplete''). |

## Manuscript hygiene (session 2026-04-22)

Manuscript is self-complete; current version stands for itself. References to previous versions, intermediate ansätze, drafting history, or bookkeeping scaffolding are deleted or rewritten as direct mathematics. The canonical statement inscribed across all three volumes lives in the *Writing standard: Chriss--Ginzburg north star* section of `CLAUDE.md` (Vol I, Vol II, Vol III): every section title names a mathematical object; every meta-narration of the author's intent is deleted; bookkeeping vocabulary (Wave~$N$, AP~$n$, DNA strand, cache entry, CG-rectify pass) is confined to `notes/`, commits, and `memory/`. This subsection enumerates the patterns rectified in the 2026-04-22 pass.

### A. Bookkeeping vocabulary

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Wave-$N$ marker | ``Wave~15 inscribed the Serre-vanishing result'' | ``The Serre-vanishing result is \dots'' | The wave index is a project-management timeline, not a mathematical structure; a reader has no referent and the phrasing dates the manuscript. |
| AP-CY$n$ / AP$n$ tag | ``This follows from AP-CY7 (CoHA $\neq$ chiral)'' | ``This follows from the distinction between the $E_1$-associative CoHA and the chiral / vertex structure transported by $\Phi$'' | AP tags index a confusion registry internal to the rectification loop; the mathematical content (the distinction itself) is what the reader needs. |
| AP-CAT-$N$ tag | ``AP-CAT-3 obstructs the direct identification'' | ``The morphism-level obstruction is the $R$-matrix-gauge equivalence class \dots'' | Category-project tags are interchangeable with the underlying obstruction; name the obstruction. |
| FM$n$ footnote | ``See FM24 for the formula-mechanical pattern'' | ``The formula obeys the Künneth multiplicative identity \dots'' | FM indices belong to a formula-mechanical registry, not to mathematics; state the identity directly. |
| HZ-$N$ / HZ-IV protocol tag | ``By the HZ-IV disjoint-verification protocol\dots'' | ``Three independent verification paths agree: (i) \dots; (ii) \dots; (iii) \dots'' | HZ protocol names a process; the reader needs the three paths, not the verification discipline. |
| DNA strand S$x$ | ``DNA strand S5 inscribed the Borcherds weight identity'' | ``The Borcherds weight identity $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_N) = c_N(0)/2$ is \dots'' | DNA-strand tags are adversarial-swarm provenance; the identity is the content. |
| CG-rectify pass $k$ | ``After the third CG-rectify pass the chapter\dots'' | ``The chapter establishes \dots'' | Meta-commentary on the rectification loop narrates process rather than mathematics. |
| Cache entry $n$ / Cached Confusion \#N | ``Cached Confusion \#46 flags this trap'' | ``The trap is: CoHA is $E_1$-associative, whereas vertex-operator chirality is transported only through $\Phi$'' | The cache entry is a pointer; the reader needs the content pointed to. |
| Cache anchor / Cache append | ``Cache append 2026-04-20 records\dots'' | (delete, move to \texttt{notes/}) | Appending records is bookkeeping; the manuscript carries the mathematical result, not its logging metadata. |
| Wave~$N$ spec / verdict / witnessing | ``Wave~13 verdict: $c_{4d} = 107/6$ witnessed by Chacaltana--Distler'' | ``$c_{4d} = 107/6$ (Chacaltana--Distler 2010 Table~3; Beem--Lemos--Peelaers--Rastelli 2015 eq.~2.27)'' | The verdict / witnessing vocabulary narrates the adjudication loop; cite the primary source for the numerical value. |
| Programme-canonical | ``The programme-canonical value of $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{10})$ is $5$'' | ``The canonical value of $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{10})$ is $5$'' | ``Programme-canonical'' introduces an in-group modifier; ``canonical'' suffices for reader-facing prose. |
| Type-error registry entry T$n$ | ``Type-error T12 catches this at the $E_n$-level'' | ``At the $E_n$-level, the operadic arities disagree: \dots'' | The registry pointer replaces the mathematical statement it was built to flag. |

### B. Meta-narration

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Narrative counterpart / arc | ``The narrative counterpart of the CY-A equivalence is \dots'' | ``The CY-A equivalence pairs with the chiral side via \dots'' | ``Narrative'' imports literary structure where the argument is the mathematical content. |
| Story / saga / odyssey / journey | ``The saga of $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ resolves through TCFT'' | ``The identity $[m_3, B^{(2)}] = 0$ holds via the Costello TCFT total-derivative \dots'' | Narrative arc language dramatises the derivation; the theorem is timeless. |
| Platonic ideal / form / chapter / architecture / ensemble / synthesis | ``The platonic ideal of this chapter is \dots'' | ``This chapter establishes \dots'' | ``Platonic'' is a meta-commentary on the author's intent; replace with the theorem the chapter proves. |
| Platonic Theorem~A | ``Platonic Theorem~A (CY-A_3 equivalence)'' | ``Theorem~A (CY-A$_3$ equivalence)'' | The modifier is editorial aspiration, not part of the theorem statement. |
| ``This chapter's function is to\dots'' | ``This chapter's function is to assemble the six routes to $G(K3\times E)$'' | ``Six constructions produce $G(K3\times E)$: (i) \dots; (ii) \dots; \dots'' | A chapter's function is realised by its content; state the content. |
| Meta-transition phrase | ``we now turn to'' / ``having established'' / ``let us now'' / ``this brings us to'' | Delete or replace with direct mathematical statement (``By the preceding proposition, \dots'') | Transitions that signpost the author's progress carry no mathematical content; an equal reader sees the logical force directly. |
| First-person programme language | ``in the present work'' / ``the author'' / ``our programme'' / ``we have argued'' / ``it is worth noting'' | Third-person / object-level: ``Here $\Phi$ denotes \dots''; ``The construction \dots''; strike ``it is worth noting'' (if worth noting, note it) | First-person programme language dates the manuscript and imports memoir; the mathematics speaks for itself. |
| Closing meta-paragraph | ``This chapter closes the \dots programme'' | Delete; let the last theorem and its proof serve as the closing | Closing meta-paragraphs editorialise what the mathematics has already done. |
| Preface self-reference | ``the opening paragraphs of this preface'' / ``the preface's close'' | Delete; direct reference to the actual theorem or construction | Self-reference to preface structure narrates pagination, not mathematics. |
| Drafting-timeline reference | ``Earlier in the volume'' / ``at earlier drafts'' | ``Chapter~$n$ established \dots'' (by chapter reference, not by timeline) | Draft history is memoir; chapter cross-references are the reader-facing location. |

### C. Version-history commentary

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Retracted / retraction | ``The retracted value $c_{2d} = -312$\dots'' | Delete the retracted value; if a naive contrast is pedagogically useful: ``A bulk-only anomaly formula gives $c_{4d} = 26$, $c_{2d} = -312$; the all-max-regular-corrected formula yields $c_{4d} = 107/6$, $c_{2d} = -214$.'' | The manuscript is self-complete; a value the current version does not assert has no reader-facing role. |
| Now retracted | ``(now retracted)'' parenthetical | Delete the parenthetical and the value it qualifies | ``Now'' dates the manuscript to the rectification session. |
| Superseded / supersedes | ``This supersedes the earlier $Y_\hbar(\osp(4\mid 20))$ identification'' | State the current identification $Y_\hbar(\so(4, 20))$ directly, with the form-structure argument | ``Supersedes'' narrates replacement; state what the replacement is. |
| Earlier draft / previous version / intermediate ansatz / prior derivation | ``The earlier draft conjectured $D_K = 18^{K-1}$'' | ``A naive exponential ansatz $D_K = 18^{K-1}$ would predict $D_3 = 324$; the direct value $D_3 = 972$ rules it out.'' | Cache entry~11: reorient timeline-comparison to naive-candidate comparison, which is timeless and reader-facing. |
| Previously conjectural / open / unresolved / obstructing | ``Previously obstructing CY-C is now resolved\dots'' | ``CY-C is resolved via three converging routes: \dots'' | ``Previously'' encodes a timeline the reader does not have access to. |
| Stray ``now'' in resolution | ``\dots is now resolved by \dots'' | ``\dots is resolved by \dots'' | Drop the timeline qualifier; the resolution stands on its own. |
| Double-retraction / successive evaluations | ``Three successive evaluations appear in the drafting record: $-107$, $-312$, $-214$'' | State the current value and its four independent factorisations directly | Drafting record is memoir; factorisation witnesses stand on their own. |
| Drafting record / trajectory | ``The drafting trajectory converged on \dots'' | Delete; state the converged result | Convergence is process; the result is mathematics. |
| \texttt{\textbackslash ClaimStatusRetracted} | \texttt{\textbackslash ClaimStatusRetracted\{\dots\}} inline marker | Delete the marker; delete the retracted claim from the chapter (move to \texttt{notes/} adjudication ledger) | A retracted claim-status marker in reader-facing prose foregrounds the retraction, not the mathematics. |
| Dated remarks | ``Remark (2026-04-17): Etingof's correction on 2026-04-19 \dots'' | Delete the date and attribution line; fold the mathematical content of the correction into the main text | Session dates and adjudicator attributions are loop metadata. |
| \texttt{\textbackslash index\{retraction!\dots\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash index\{retraction!osp/so\}} in \texttt{.tex} source | Delete the index entry | The index should guide to mathematics, not to the retraction history. |

### D. Bookkeeping-content references

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| \texttt{\textbackslash texttt\{notes/*\}} citations | ``See \texttt{notes/wave13\_a3\_gkm\_presentation\_etingof.tex}'' | Cite the primary literature (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1995, Borcherds 1998); if a chapter elaborates, cite the chapter or theorem label | \texttt{notes/} is repository-internal scaffolding; the reader-facing artefact is the published source and the manuscript theorem. |
| Absolute paths in comments | \texttt{\% See /Users/raeez/calabi-yau-quantum-groups/notes/\dots} | Delete the path; cite the primary source or the theorem label | File-system paths are author-local; they leak working-directory metadata into the manuscript. |
| \texttt{\% TODO: librarian verification} | \texttt{\% TODO: librarian verify Gritsenko--Nikulin page ref} | Perform the verification; if pending, add \texttt{\textbackslash ClaimStatusConjectured} to the inline claim | TODO markers in reader-facing \texttt{.tex} signal unfinished work; status tags are the reader-visible mechanism. |
| \texttt{\% ALIAS} / \texttt{\% LEGACY ALIAS} | \texttt{\% ALIAS: old-label-name} in \texttt{bibliography/references.tex} | Remove the alias comment; retain only the canonical BibTeX entry | Aliases are versioning bookkeeping internal to the bib file. |
| \texttt{\% Source: NEW CHAPTER (see notes/\dots)} | Editorial comment recording provenance of a chapter insertion | Delete the comment; the chapter's content is its provenance | Provenance of insertion is git-tracked, not reader-facing. |
| Compute-engine filenames | Prose citation of \texttt{compute/lib/k3\_yangian\_wave14\_arthur\_hecke\_delta10.py} | Cite the mathematical construction (``The Arthur--Hecke $\Delta_{10}$ lift\dots''); relegate the filename to a repository \texttt{README.md} / \texttt{compute/} index | Internal compute-engine names encode wave metadata; the reader-facing artefact is the construction. |
| Internal Python function names | \texttt{wave14\_arthur\_hecke\_delta10()} in prose | Cite the function's mathematical output (``the Arthur--Hecke lift'') | Python function names are implementation, not mathematics. |

### E. Warning boxes and hedge language

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| \texttt{\textbackslash begin\{warning\}}\dots\texttt{\textbackslash end\{warning\}} | ``\textbackslash begin\{warning\} Do not confuse CoHA with chiral \dots \textbackslash end\{warning\}'' | Replace with a definition or remark stating the two structures and the precise distinction: ``Two structures, two different categories: $\mathrm{CoHA}$ is an $E_1$-associative algebra (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2011); the chiral / vertex structure is produced by $\Phi_{\mathcal C}$.'' | Warning boxes foreground the reader's potential error; the CG standard is to state the correct mathematics directly and let its precision prevent confusion. |
| ``do not confuse'' / ``don't be fooled'' / ``beware'' / ``be careful'' | ``Do not confuse the Mukai rank $24$ with the Cartan rank $3$.'' | ``The Mukai rank is $24$; the Cartan rank is $3$; these are two distinct invariants attached to two distinct structures.'' | Hedge imperatives patronise the reader; direct statements carry the same content without the scolding. |
| ``we must be careful'' | ``We must be careful: $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = 0$, not $2$.'' | ``$\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E}) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal O_E) = 2\cdot 0 = 0$.'' | The Künneth identity is the explanation; the hedge ``be careful'' adds nothing. |
| Gratuitous scope-restricted | ``On compact CY$_3$ (strictly; scope-restricted), $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$'' | ``On compact CY$_3$, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} = \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(X)/24$'' | The scope is already named (``compact CY$_3$''); ``strictly; scope-restricted'' is empty hedging. |
| ``Verdict'' as meta-label | ``Verdict: the identity fails at $N = 3$'' | ``The identity fails at $N = 3$: direct evaluation gives $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_3) = 3 \neq c_3(0)/2 + \chi(\mathcal O_{E}) = 3 + 0 = 3$'' | ``Verdict'' is adjudication-loop vocabulary; state the failure and its witness. |

### F. Structural meta-naming

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| Chapter filename \texttt{\_platonic} | \texttt{chapters/examples/k3\_chiral\_bialgebra\_platonic.tex} | \texttt{chapters/examples/k3\_chiral\_bialgebra.tex} | The filename is reader-visible in repository navigation; ``platonic'' is editorial aspiration and should not survive into the filesystem artefact. |
| Chapter label \texttt{ch:*-platonic} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra\}} | Labels are cited in cross-references throughout the manuscript; a ``platonic'' suffix propagates the meta-modifier into every reference. |
| Section label \texttt{sec:*-platonic} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{sec:cy-c-three-routes-platonic\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{sec:cy-c-three-routes\}} | Same propagation concern as chapter labels. |
| Theorem label \texttt{thm:*-waveN-*} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{thm:w15-phi-arrow-discipline\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash label\{thm:phi-arrow-discipline\}} | Wave indices in theorem labels timestamp the adjudication session; every cross-reference would then carry the timestamp. |
| \texttt{\textbackslash index\{compute module!\dots\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash index\{compute module!k3\_yangian\_wave14\}} | Delete; compute modules are not a reader-facing index category | The reader index should point to mathematical concepts, not to implementation artefacts. |
| \texttt{\textbackslash index\{cache!\dots\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash index\{cache!entry 46\}} | Delete | Cache is a rectification-loop registry; not a reader navigation axis. |
| \texttt{\textbackslash index\{retraction!\dots\}} | \texttt{\textbackslash index\{retraction!osp-vs-so\}} | Delete; the retracted claim itself is removed from the chapter | The index should not expose retraction history. |

### G. Edge cases

| Pattern name | Forbidden form (example) | Canonical replacement | Mathematical reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| ``Five attack-heal calibrations'' | ``Five attack-heal calibrations fix the $R$-matrix sign \dots'' | ``Five independent calibrations fix the $R$-matrix sign: \dots'' or enumerate the calibrations directly | ``Attack-heal'' is adversarial-swarm vocabulary; ``independent calibrations'' is the reader-facing concept. |
| ``Reconstitution if the cancellation fails'' remark title | Remark titled ``Reconstitution if the cancellation fails'' | Remark titled by the actual mathematical content: ``Alternative assembly when the $[m_3, B^{(2)}]$ cancellation fails termwise'' | ``Reconstitution'' is a process word; the reader needs the mathematical alternative. |
| ``Inversion of the programme perspective'' | ``Inversion of the programme perspective: read $\Phi$ right-to-left'' | ``Dual perspective: $\Phi$ right-to-left identifies $(A, B(A), A^!, Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(A))$ starting from \dots'' | ``Inversion of the programme perspective'' narrates a meta-move; ``Dual perspective'' names the mathematical structure. |
| ``History of the claim'' | Subsection titled ``History of the claim'' | Delete the subsection (move to \texttt{notes/} adjudication ledger) or retitle with mathematical content | The manuscript carries the current statement and its proof; claim history belongs in rectification notes. |
| ``Gold-standard HZ-IV disjoint verification'' | ``Proved to gold-standard HZ-IV disjoint verification'' | ``Three independent verification paths agree (direct computation; class-$\mathcal S$ anomaly-formula evaluation; Borcherds denominator expansion)'' | ``Gold-standard'' is rank; ``HZ-IV'' is protocol; name the three paths. |
| ``Three successive evaluations appear in the drafting record'' | Remark opening ``Three successive evaluations appear in the drafting record'' | ``Three independent evaluations give the value $c_{4d} = 107/6$: (i) \dots; (ii) \dots; (iii) \dots'' | ``Successive'' is temporal (timeline); ``independent'' is logical (epistemic weight). Reader needs the latter. |


## Wave 14 session-correction cache append (2026-04-22, AP-CY196--202)

Second session-correction batch from Wave 12--14 inflight adjudication. Seven patterns caught during Igusa / Gritsenko--Cl\'ery / Lorgat-2020 inscription rounds; complements the six Wave-14 conceptual patterns (AP-CY160--165) above. AP-CY166--195 taken by concurrent Fleets A/B/C/D and main-chapter Beilinson sweeps; this batch continues at AP-CY196. Covers: non-isolated critical locus ($W = xyz$) vs Ginzburg dg-algebra presentation of non-commutative $\mathbb C^3$; CY$_d$-linear Morita invariance vs bare dg-Morita; four distinct readings of ``DT zeta roots'' in Lorgat 2020 Conjecture 1; Humbert discriminant $n_i = \det(\Lambda_{K3}^{g_{N_i}})$ vs CHL orbifold level $N_i$; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 8-form atlas vs CHL 5-level enumeration (two scopes for $\kBKM$); the ``10 real simple roots'' conflation in $\fg_{\Delta_5}$; and the $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ chiral-half vs full-dyonic-BPS discipline.

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| W14-B1 | $\mathrm{MF}(xyz) = \mathbb C^3$ via Kn\"orrer periodicity (essay slogan quoting matrix-factorisation folklore). | $\mathbb C^3$ as a dg-category arises from a genuine non-commutative CY$_3$ datum: the Ginzburg dg-algebra $\Gamma(Q_{\mathbb C^3}, W_{\mathbb C^3})$ is a CY$_3$-complete enhancement of $\mathrm{Coh}(\mathbb C^3)$. | $W = xyz$ on $\mathbb C^3$ has NON-ISOLATED critical locus: $\mathrm{Crit}(W) = \{x = y = 0\} \cup \{y = z = 0\} \cup \{x = z = 0\}$ (three coordinate axes), so Kn\"orrer periodicity FAILS (Kn\"orrer 1987 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 88 requires isolated singularity). The slogan conflates the target identification with the source mechanism. | The correct CY$_3$ datum on non-commutative $\mathbb C^3$ is the Ginzburg dg-algebra $\Gamma(Q_{\mathbb C^3}, W_{\mathbb C^3})$ with $Q_{\mathbb C^3}$ the three-loop quiver (one vertex, arrows $x, y, z$) and $W_{\mathbb C^3} = xyz - xzy$; Jacobi algebra $J(Q, W) = \mathbb C\langle x, y, z\rangle / (\partial_x W, \partial_y W, \partial_z W) = \mathbb C[x, y, z]$ (cyclic derivatives = commutators). Primary: Ginzburg 2006 \texttt{arXiv:math/0612139} \S 4.2; Schiffmann--Vasserot 2013 \emph{Publ.\ IH\'ES} 118 (on $\mathrm{CoHA}(\mathbb C^3) = Y^+$); Kn\"orrer 1987 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 88 Thm 3.1 (isolated-singularity hypothesis); Orlov 2009 \emph{Progr.\ Math.} 270 \S 3. | AP-CY196 / MF($xyz$) non-isolated critical locus vs Ginzburg-dg CY$_3$ datum |
| W14-B2 | Morita-invariant derived equivalence automatically transports CY$_d$ structure: any dg-Morita equivalence of $(\mathcal C, \eta)$ with $(\mathcal C', \eta')$ preserves the trace. | Morita-invariance of the BARE dg-category $\mathcal C$ (without trace) is automatic: dg-Morita $\mathcal C \simeq \mathcal C'$ induces $\HH_\bullet(\mathcal C) \cong \HH_\bullet(\mathcal C')$ by Keller 1998 \emph{Manuscr.\ Math.} 95. | Morita-invariance of the PAIR $(\mathcal C, \eta)$ requires the equivalence to be CY$_d$-LINEAR: the equivalence $F\colon \mathcal C \to \mathcal C'$ must satisfy $F^* \eta' = \eta$ as classes in $\HH_{-d}(\mathcal C)$. A generic dg-Morita equivalence transports $\HH_\bullet$ but not necessarily the specific class $\eta \in \HH_{-d}$; the trace lift is an additional datum. | CY$_d$-linear $\Leftrightarrow$ Serre-functor-intertwining $\Leftrightarrow$ ChirHoch$_{-d}$-pairing isomorphism: $F\colon \mathcal C \to \mathcal C'$ is CY$_d$-linear iff $F \circ S_{\mathcal C} \simeq S_{\mathcal C'} \circ F[d]$ (Serre functor intertwiner) iff $F^*\eta' = \eta$ (ChirHoch pairing iso). Bridgeland 2002 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.} 11 conifold flop $D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X_+) \simeq D^b\mathrm{Coh}(X_-)$ is CY$_3$-linear because both sides share the common NCCR $\Lambda_{\mathrm{NCCR}} = \End_R(R \oplus I)$ (Van den Bergh 2004 \emph{Duke} 122 Thm A), and the NCCR trace intertwines both Serre pairings. Primary: Keller 1998 \emph{Manuscr.\ Math.} 95 Thm 4.3; Bridgeland 2002 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.} 11; Van den Bergh 2004 \emph{Duke} 122 Thm A; Kuznetsov 2007 \emph{arXiv:math/0702842} \S 2.7 (CY-linear functor definition); Kontsevich--Soibelman 2009 \texttt{arXiv:0906.0996} \S 4.1. | AP-CY197 / CY$_d$-linear Morita invariance vs bare dg-Morita |
| W14-B3 | ``DT zeta roots'' in Lorgat 2020 Conjecture 1 is unambiguous: the eight roots of $Z^X_{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{red}}$ correspond bijectively to eight Gritsenko--Cl\'ery forms. | $Z^X_{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{red}}$ and its reciprocal-square-root paramodular lift do both exist and are interrelated via Borcherds-product structure (Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.} 27). | Four distinct readings of ``DT zeta roots'' coexist, not individuated by the phrase alone: (1) functional zeros of $Z^X_{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{red}}$ as a function on $\mathcal A_2^*$; (2) poles of the denominator $\prod \Delta_k$; (3) reciprocal-square-root $F_i = (Z^X_{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{red}})^{-1/2}$ as a paramodular form; (4) Humbert-divisor locus $H_{n_i} \subset \mathcal A_2^*$ where a specific $F_i$ vanishes. Conjecture 1 only makes sense under readings (3)+(4). | Readings (3)+(4) are the intended content: $F_i$ is the reciprocal-square-root of $Z^X_{\mathrm{DT}, \mathrm{red}}$, and $\mathrm{div}(F_i) = H_{n_i} \subset \mathcal A_2^*$ is a Humbert divisor with discriminant $n_i$. Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.} 27 Thm 1 proves the $N = 1$ case (K3 $\times$ E) unconditionally; $N \geq 2$ CHL cases conditional on BOPY 2018 \texttt{arXiv:1802.07973} CHL-reduced DT conjecture. Every inscription invoking ``DT zeta roots'' must specify which reading; reading (1) or (2) alone misses the Humbert-divisor content. Primary: Lorgat 2020 \texttt{arXiv:2004.09030} Conjecture 1 \S 4; Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2018 \emph{J.\ Alg.\ Geom.} 27 Thm 1; Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin 2018 \texttt{arXiv:1802.07973} (CHL-reduced DT); Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2018 \emph{Manuscripta Math.} 156 (paramodular atlas). | AP-CY198 / ``DT zeta roots'' four readings and paramodular reciprocal-square-root semantics |
| W14-B4 | Humbert discriminant $n_i$ equals the CHL level $N_i$: the Humbert divisor index and the CHL orbifold level are the same integer. | At $N_i \in \{1, 3, 4\}$ the numerical coincidence $n_i = N_i^2$ holds: $(n_1, n_3, n_4) = (1, 9, 16) = (1^2, 3^2, 4^2)$. | The definitions are incompatible: $n_i = \det(\Lambda_{K3}^{g_{N_i}})$ is the determinant of the \emph{symplectic $g_{N_i}$-fixed sublattice} of the K3 Mukai lattice $\Lambda_{K3} = \mathrm{II}_{4, 20}$, NOT the CHL orbifold level $N_i$. The coincidence $n_i = N_i^2$ at $N_i \in \{1, 3, 4\}$ is a byproduct of the Mukai 1988 classification of symplectic automorphisms (Mukai 1988 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 94), and FAILS at $N_i \in \{2, 5, 7, 8\}$: $(n_2, n_5, n_7, n_8) = (2, \text{not } 25, \ldots)$. | Humbert discriminant $n_i$ is lattice-determinantal data; CHL level $N_i$ is orbifold/quotient-order data. Correct formula: $n_i = \det(\Lambda_{K3}^{g_{N_i}})$ where $g_{N_i}$ runs over symplectic-automorphism generators of Mukai's classification. At $N_i \in \{1, 3, 4\}$ the coincidence $n_i = N_i^2$ arises because the symplectic-fixed sublattice has rank $2$ at those $N$; at $N_i \in \{2, 5, 7, 8\}$ rank and determinant decouple from $N_i$. Never substitute $n_i$ for $N_i$ in formulas indexing Gritsenko--Cl\'ery forms. Primary: Mukai 1988 \emph{Invent.\ Math.} 94 \S 1; Nikulin 1979 \emph{Izv.\ Akad.\ Nauk SSSR} 43; Gritsenko--Hulek 1998 \emph{Algebr.\ Geom.} \S 1 (Humbert discriminant); Cl\'ery--Gritsenko 2013 \emph{J.\ Reine Angew.\ Math.} 678. | AP-CY199 / Humbert discriminant $n_i$ vs CHL level $N_i$ dimension/index mismatch |
| W14-B5 | The 8 Gritsenko--Cl\'ery paramodular forms and the 8 order-indexed CHL/Nikulin entries enumerate the same sibling BKM family. | Both families are small paramodular lists attached to K3 automorphisms. | Different indexing principles: Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 classifies the 8 dd-modular forms by diagonal-divisor paramodular type $(t,N;k)$, while CHL enumerates by orbifold order. The two indexings agree only at $\Delta_5=(1,1;5)$ unless a separate overlap theorem is supplied. | Two-scope formulation: \textbf{(A) BKM-denominator scope} --- CHL family $N \in \{1, 2, 3, 4, 6\}$ gives Borcherds-lift denominators with $\kBKM=c_N(0)/2\in\{5,3,2,\tfrac32,1\}$ and $c_N(0)=(10,6,4,3,2)$ (Jatkar--Sen 2006; Govindarajan--Krishna arXiv:0907.1410). \textbf{(B) Gritsenko--Cl\'ery dd-modular scope} --- the eight triples $(1,1;5),(2,1;2),(1,2;3),(3,1;1),(1,3;2),(4,1;\tfrac12),(1,4;\tfrac32),(2,2;1)$ have weights $(5,2,3,1,2,\tfrac12,\tfrac32,1)$ and twice-weights $(10,4,6,2,4,1,3,2)$. No weight-$0$ row, no weight-$1/4$ row, and no double-metaplectic-cover row occurs. Primary: CHL 1995; Gritsenko 1999; Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 Theorems 1.4 and 3.1. | AP-CY200 / Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 8-form atlas vs CHL enumeration |
| W14-B6 | The BKM superalgebra $\fg_{\Delta_5}$ has ``10 real simple roots'' as read from Lorgat 2020 \S 4. | The integer 10 appears twice in the Igusa/Gritsenko data for $\Delta_5$: once as $c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0, 0) = \phi_{0,1}(0, 0) = 10$ (the Borcherds weight input at the origin Fourier coefficient), and once as $|\mathcal T| = 10$ (the cardinality of even theta constants on $\mathbb H_2$ in the factorisation $\Delta_5 = \prod_{(a,b) \in \mathcal T} \nu_{a, b}$). | Neither 10 is a count of real simple roots. The actual count is 3: $\fg_{\Delta_5}$ has THREE real primitive simple roots $\{\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3\}$ on the hyperbolic core $\Lambda^{2, 1}_{II}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke} 94 Thm 2.1, Cartan matrix $a_{ij} = 2\delta_{ij} - 2$, Gram $\det = -32$). The 10 is a \emph{conflation trap}: reading ``10 simples'' from $c(0,0) = 10$ or from 10 theta constants misses the $\Lambda^{2,1}$ core rank. | Three real primitive simples $\{\delta_1, \delta_2, \delta_3\}$; ten is $c_{\phi_{0,1}}(0, 0) = 10$ (Borcherds weight-input Fourier coefficient) and $|\mathcal T| = 10$ (even theta-constant cardinality), never a real-simple-root count. The even theta-constant count factors as $|\mathcal T| = \binom{2 \cdot 2 + 1}{1} \cdot \binom{2}{1} = 10$ via even characteristics $(a, b) \in (\frac 1 2 \mathbb Z / \mathbb Z)^4$ with $a \cdot b \equiv 0 \pmod 2$. Primary: Lorgat 2020 \texttt{arXiv:2004.09030} \S 4 eq.\ 4.7 (``$\Delta_5 = \prod \nu_{a,b}$'' and $\phi_{0,1}(0, 0) = 10$); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke} 94 Thm 2.1; Igusa 1964 \emph{Am.\ J.\ Math.} 86; Borcherds 1988 \emph{J.\ Alg.} 115 Def.\ 1 (GKM axioms). See Vol III canonical preamble row ``K3-BKM Cartan rank = 3'' (\texttt{notes/antipatterns\_catalogue.md}) and \texttt{chapters/examples/cy\_d\_kappa\_stratification.tex}. | AP-CY201 / ten-conflation trap: $c(0,0)$ vs $|\mathcal T|$ vs real-simple-root count |
| W14-B7 | $\Delta_5$ and $\Phi_{10}$ are ``the same BPS generating function'' up to normalisation, so $\kBKM(\Delta_5) = \kBKM(\Phi_{10})$. | The relationship $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ between Igusa cusp form and paramodular Gritsenko form is a genuine square identity (Gritsenko 1994 \emph{St.\ Petersburg Math.\ J.} 6 \S 3). | Chiral-half vs full-dyonic-BPS discipline: $\Delta_5$ (Siegel weight 5, \textbf{order-2 multiplier} $\nu_{\Delta_5}$, paramodular weight 5) is the CHIRAL HALF Borcherds lift of $\phi_{0, 1}$ (heterotic $1/2$-BPS); $\Phi_{10}$ (Siegel weight 10, trivial multiplier) is DVV's DYONIC $1/4$-BPS counting function $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ realising heterotic/Type-II $S$-duality at the automorphic level (Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B} 484). Equating the two $\kBKM$ drops the factor of 2 from the square. | $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ at the Siegel-form level: the square map takes the chiral-half paramodular form to the full dyonic Igusa form. Weight identity: $\kBKM(\Phi_{10}) = c_{\phi_{0, 1}}(0, 0) = 10 = 2 \cdot 5 = 2 \kBKM(\Delta_5)$. The dyonic square DOUBLES the chiral weight, consistent with the $1/2$-BPS $\to$ $1/4$-BPS promotion under heterotic/Type-II duality. Every inscription must name which form (chiral-half $\Delta_5$ or full-dyonic $\Phi_{10}$); substituting one for the other breaks Gritsenko 1999 Thm 6.1 ($\kBKM = c_N(0)/2$) and contradicts the canonical preamble ``K3-BKM Weyl denominator $= \Delta_5$'' lock (row 23 of \texttt{notes/antipatterns\_catalogue.md}). Primary: Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 \emph{Nucl.\ Phys.\ B} 484 \S 3 (dyonic $1/4$-BPS); Gritsenko 1994 \emph{St.\ Petersburg Math.\ J.} 6 \S 3; Gritsenko 1999 \emph{Math.\ Nachr.} 199 Thm 6.1 ($\kBKM = c_N(0)/2$); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \emph{Duke} 94 Thm 2.1; Lorgat 2020 \texttt{arXiv:2004.09030} \S 4. | AP-CY202 / $\Phi_{10} = \Delta_5^2$ chiral-half vs full-dyonic-BPS discipline |

## Waves 11--19 numerical + retraction confusion-pattern append (2026-04-22)

Six numerical errors (Num1--Num6) and nine retractions (Ret1--Ret9) from the adversarial audit of the Vol III K3 chiral bialgebra construction. Each row pairs with an AP-CY entry in \texttt{notes/antipatterns\_catalogue.md}: Num1--Num6 $\to$ AP-CY190--AP-CY195; Ret1--Ret9 $\to$ AP-CY166, 167, 168, 169, 170, 171, 172, 173, 175. The constructive counterparts (positive-structure statements) are rows C1--C12 above.

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| Num1 | $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}(K3 \times E) = 2$. | $\chi(\mathcal O_{K3}) = 2$ is a genuine fibre Hodge number. | Conflates the K3-fibre contribution with total-space \(\chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E})\). K\"unneth is multiplicative on products of compact CY: \(\chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E})=2\cdot0=0\). | \(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3\times E)=0\), \(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E)=0\), \(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3\), and \(\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}=24\). Always name the \(\kappa_\bullet\)-index. | fibre vs total-space $\kappa_\bullet$ |
| Num2 | Central charge $= -14432/121$. | $-1312/11$ central charge of the Vol III landmark computed by two independent routes. | Rational arithmetic miscombination of two contributions; $-14432/121$ is a non-reduced representation of a different intermediate. Direct OPE + Virasoro bootstrap both give $-1312/11$. | $-1312/11$ via two independent routes: direct OPE + Virasoro bootstrap $c_{2d} = -12 c_{4d}$ (Beem-Rastelli). Primary: direct OPE; Beem-Rastelli 2013 \emph{Commun Math Phys} 336. Cross-ref C9 (Gaiotto curve correction); AP-CY130, AP-CY46, AP-CY167. | AP-CY191 / central-charge arithmetic discipline |
| Num3 | $(1, 48, 1176, 19456, \dots)$ is a Virasoro $(p, q)$-minimal-model coefficient sequence. | Heisenberg partition function on a K3-derived chiral structure. | Missing the $\eta^{-48}$ Heisenberg-Mukai identity: $\eta(\tau)^{-48} = q^{-2}(1 + 48 q + 1176 q^2 + 19456 q^3 + \cdots)$ to all orders. 48 = $2 \cdot 24 = 2\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3)$. | $(1, 48, 1176, 19456, \dots)$ is the $\eta^{-48}$ Heisenberg-Mukai identity, not a Virasoro minimal-model sequence. Test against four canonical $\eta$-power expansions ($\eta^{-48}$ K3 Heisenberg, $\eta^{-24}$ Monster/Leech, $\eta^{-8}$ Enriques, $\eta^{-12}$ quarter-twined) before Macdonald-framework invocation. Primary: Mukai 1987 \emph{Nagoya Math J} 108; Kac 1990 \emph{Infinite-Dimensional Lie Algebras} Ch 12. Cross-ref C10 (Heisenberg-Mukai $\eta^{-48}$ identity). | AP-CY192 / $\eta^{-48}$ Heisenberg-Mukai vs minimal-model |
| Num4 | Virasoro $(2, 45)$-minimal-model Macdonald framework applies to the Vol III K3 chiral analysis. | Macdonald identities apply on affine Kac-Moody characters; minimal models have $(p, q)$ Kac labels + degenerate primaries. | K3 Heisenberg target has Mukai-lattice-indexed primaries, not Virasoro primaries; OPE singularity structure abelian in Fock sector; $c = 1 - 6(p - q)^2/pq$ has no integer $(p, q)$ solution at the K3 target central charge. | Before invoking Macdonald / minimal-model framework, verify: (a) Virasoro-Kac primary decomposition; (b) OPE singularity match against Kac-table fusion rules; (c) null-state level at $pq$. K3 Heisenberg target fails all three. Primary: Di Francesco-Mathieu-S\'en\'echal 1997 \emph{Conformal Field Theory} Ch 7; Macdonald 1972 \emph{Invent Math} 15. Cross-ref AP-CY117 (abelian / non-abelian Yangian discipline); AP-CY170. | AP-CY193 / Macdonald / minimal-model scope applicability |
| Num5 | $\mathrm{wt}(\Delta^{(3)}_1)=3$ by catalogue position. | The Gritsenko--Cl\'ery catalogue enumerates reflective dd-modular products. | Position index is not the Borcherds weight. The eight weights are $(5,2,3,1,2,\tfrac12,\tfrac32,1)$ in the triple order $(1,1),(2,1),(1,2),(3,1),(1,3),(4,1),(1,4),(2,2)$. Position 3 has weight 3 because its triple is $(1,2;3)$, not because the position number is 3. | Quote the triple $(t,N;k)$ and the weight $k$ together. The only non-integral weights in the catalogue are $\tfrac12$ and $\tfrac32$, both carried by multiplier systems; there is no weight-$0$ or weight-$1/4$ row. Primary: Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008 arXiv:0812.3962 Theorems 1.4 and 3.1. | AP-CY194 / 8-form position vs Borcherds weight |
| Num6 | $c_2(0) = 8$ uniformly across twining conventions. | 8-form Gritsenko-Cl\'ery Fourier coefficients index $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = c_N(0)/2$. | Doubly-twined convention absorbs factor of 2 from K3 elliptic genus normalisation $Z^{(g)}_{K3} = 2 \phi^{(g)}_{0, 1}$: $c_2(0) = 8$ doubly, $c_2(0) = 4$ singly. Cross-volume citations must state convention. | Doubly-twined $c_2(0) = 8 \Rightarrow \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 4$; singly-twined $c_2(0) = 4 \Rightarrow \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}} = 2$. Always name convention at point of use. Extends AP-CY125 (EOT factor-of-two) and AP-CY49 (cross-volume $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ dual-indexing). Primary: EOT 2011 \emph{Exper Math} 20 Thm 1.1; Gritsenko-Cl\'ery 2008 arXiv:0812.3962. | AP-CY195 / twined/untwined convention discipline |

### 2026-04-22: 15 further cache entries from Fleets A/B/C/D integration wave (AP-CY174, 176--189)

The fifteen entries below complete the Vol III cache mirror of the Fleets A/B/C/D integration-wave catalogue (`notes/antipatterns_catalogue.md` AP-CY166 through AP-CY189). Prior cache rows Ret1--Ret9 above cover AP-CY166--173 and AP-CY175; the entries here cover the Hecke-trace misread (AP-CY174), the Super-Yangian envelope trichotomy (AP-CY176), the six-routes-as-$\Phi_3$ recurrence (AP-CY177), the six structural/LaTeX integration-wave errors (AP-CY178--183), the two voice/style recurrences (AP-CY184--185), the one cross-volume propagation entry (AP-CY186), and the three process-level failure modes (AP-CY187--189).

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |
|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|----------------------|------|
| Ret10 | $\mathrm{Tr}(T_p) = 1$ on the one-dimensional $S_5(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z), \nu_{\Delta_5}) = \mathbb C \cdot \Delta_5$ by multiplicity-one uniqueness of $\Delta_5$. | Multiplicity-one fixes the eigenspace to one dimension; the trace on a one-dimensional eigenspace equals the eigenvalue. | The trace on a one-dimensional Hecke eigenspace equals the Hecke eigenvalue $\lambda_p(\Delta_5)$, NOT $1$. Multiplicity-one controls eigenspace \emph{dimension}, not the eigenvalue. | $\mathrm{Tr}(T_p) = \lambda_p(\Delta_5) = \chi_{\mathrm{spin}}(p) \sqrt{\lambda_p(\Delta_{10})}$ via the Saito--Kurokawa lift $\Delta_{10} = \mathrm{SK}(\Delta_5^2)$; at $p = 2$, $\lambda_2(\Delta_5) \approx \pm \sqrt{-48} \cdot \chi_2$. Primary: Ikeda 2001 \emph{Ann Math} 154 Cor 16.2; Pitale--Saha--Schmidt 2014 \emph{Memoirs AMS} 232 \S4. Cross-ref: AP-CY35 (Chenevier determinant); FM27 (Saito--Kurokawa spinor vs standard); AP-CY133 (Arthur parameter reducibility); AP-CY134 (Hecke Euler factor convolution primes); canonical preamble row 54 ($e_5 = W_5$ uniqueness). | AP-CY174 / multiplicity-one vs Hecke eigenvalue discipline |
| Ret11 | Hodge-parity super-extension of $Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{Heis}}(\Lambda_{K3})$ equals Kac-super $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{osp}(4|20))$. | The Mukai pairing on $H^*(K3)$ is symmetric indefinite of signature $(4, 20)$; a non-abelian super-Yangian enveloping it is a real programme target, conjectural per AP-CY117. | Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(m|n)$ requires the graded bilinear form to be symmetric on the even part and \emph{skew-symmetric} on the odd part; the Mukai pairing is symmetric on both Hodge-even $H^{\mathrm{even}}(K3)$ and Hodge-odd $H^{\mathrm{odd}}(K3)$ sectors, so neither $\mathfrak{osp}(4|20)$ (symplectic on odd) nor $\mathfrak{gl}(4|20)$ (Berezinian) applies. | Classical bosonic envelope: $\mathfrak{so}(4, 20)$ (orthogonal indefinite). Programme Hodge-parity super-extension: $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4|20))$, non-Kac, $\mathbb Z/2$-graded orthogonal Yangian with ACDFR 2003 super-Sklyanin--Berezinian reflection-equation datum; \texttt{ClaimStatusConjectured}. Sits atop the already-conjectural non-abelian lift $Y^{\mathrm{Heis}}_\hbar \to Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4, 20))$ (AP-CY117). Primary: Mukai 1987 \emph{Invent Math} 77; Kac 1977 \emph{Adv Math} 26 (explicitly excluded as scope reference); ACDFR 2003 \texttt{arXiv:math/0304188}; Schiffmann--Vasserot 2020 \emph{Publ IHES} 132. Cross-ref: AP-CY117 (K3 Yangian abelian vs non-abelian); \texttt{chapters/theory/quantum\_groups\_foundations.tex} super-Yangian scope; canonical preamble row on Mukai pairing. | AP-CY176 / Super-Yangian envelope: $\mathfrak{so}(4,20)$ classical, $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4|20))$ programme-specific non-Kac, $\mathfrak{osp}(4|20)$ / $\mathfrak{gl}(4|20)$ forbidden |
| Ret12 | The six routes (CoHA, Schiffmann--Vasserot, Maulik--Okounkov, Borcherds, Toda, DMVV) to $G(K3 \times E)$ are six applications of $\Phi_3$ to the same CY$_3$ input. | The six constructions each produce a candidate algebra that conjecturally matches $G(K3 \times E)$ via pentagon colimit over five named intertwiners $\beta_{13}, \beta_{34}, \beta_{45}, \beta_{56}, \beta_{61}$; CY-C records the conjectural coincidence. | $\Phi_3$ is a functor: $\Phi_3(\mathcal C)$ gives one output per input category $\mathcal C$, not six. The six routes take six \emph{different} input categories (CoHA on $\mathrm{Coh}(K3 \times E)$; $D^T(\mathrm{Hilb}(K3) \times E)$ for SV; stable envelopes on $T^*\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$ for MO; reflective automorphic product on $\mathrm{II}_{2, 3}$ for Borcherds; motivic DT generating function for Toda; second-quantised elliptic genus for DMVV). Functoriality would produce one output per input. | Six \emph{different constructions}, each with its own input category, converging conjecturally (CY-C, AP-CY6) on a common target $G(K3 \times E)$. This confusion recurs despite five prior AP-CY entries (AP-CY48, AP-CY81, AP-CY96, AP-CY122, AP-Vol-III-prop-4); re-surfacing is a manifesto-level risk that must be caught at inscription. Primary: Schiffmann--Vasserot 2017 \emph{Publ IHES} 118; Maulik--Okounkov 2012 \texttt{arXiv:1211.1287}; Borcherds 1995 \emph{Invent Math} 120; Toda 2014 \emph{Invent Math} 196; Dijkgraaf--Moore--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 \emph{Commun Math Phys} 185. | Struct1 | References \texttt{\textbackslash ref\{part:foundations\}}, \texttt{part:cy-to-chiral}, \texttt{part:cy-landscape}, \texttt{part:seven-faces}, \texttt{part:frontiers} and chapter refs \texttt{ch:cy-c-beyond-k3e-existence-obstruction}, \texttt{ch:k3e-bkm-chapter}, \texttt{ch:k3e-cy3-programme}, \texttt{ch:derived-categories-cy}, \texttt{ch:e1-chiral-algebras}, \texttt{ch:e2-chiral-algebras}, \texttt{ch:holographic-datum}, \texttt{chap:cy-to-chiral}, \texttt{ch:phi-universal-trace} resolve to defined labels. | Cross-references are load-bearing hyperlinks; the intended mathematical objects (Parts I--VII, key chapters) exist and are labelled. | The referenced keys do not match the defined Vol III labels \texttt{part:cy-categories}, \texttt{part:bridge}, \texttt{part:examples}, \texttt{part:connections}, \texttt{part:frontier} (Parts); and \texttt{ch:cy-c-beyond-k3e}, \texttt{ch:k3e-bkm}, \texttt{ch:k3-times-e}, \texttt{ch:derived-cy}, \texttt{ch:e1-chiral}, \texttt{ch:e2-chiral}, \texttt{ch:cy-holographic-datum-master}, \texttt{ch:cy-to-chiral}, \texttt{ch:phi-universal-trace-platonic} (chapters). Observed: two agents returned mid-sentence summaries during the integration wave, yet target files were modified as expected (verified via \texttt{git diff}). | Verify disk state via \texttt{git diff} + \texttt{grep -l} on key theorem labels; never trust the summary as a proxy for completion. | Proc3 | An agent tasked with ``rectify files $F_1, F_2, \ldots, F_n$'' will touch all $n$ files. | Agents parse multi-file task specifications and carry them out in order. | Under long-running tasks, agents complete a subset $F_1, \ldots, F_k$ with $k < n$ and return without touching the remainder, either due to context-budget exhaustion or a mid-task ``I have now completed'' hallucination. Observed: Fleet B agent tasked with 8 chapters completed 5 and returned; remaining 3 required follow-up. | After every agent run, verify the target-file set against the task specification; follow up on any uncovered files. Sweep: \texttt{for f in \$F\_1 \ldots \$F\_n; do git diff "\$f" | head -1; done}; files with empty diff are orphaned. Cross-ref: AP159 (report $\ne$ disk); AP-CY188 (truncated summary); AP-CY27 (sandbox non-persistence). | AP-CY189 / agent orphan-task (partial file coverage) |

## Wave 12 synthesis supplemental cache append (2026-04-22)

Supplemental Wave-12 rows complementing the Num1--Num6 + Ret1--Ret9 fleet entries above. Continues AP-CY numbering at 225 (skipping 176--224 already allocated to concurrent Fleets A/B/C/D and the Wave-14 session-correction batch AP-CY196--202). Covers patterns not individually catalogued: total-space $\{0,3,5,24\}$ vs fibre-mixed $\{2,3,5,24\}$, six-routes vs six-$\Phi$-applications, CY-A$_3$ equivalence-vs-existence, CoHA$\simeq\mathcal W_{1+\infty}$ evaluation image, Hecke trace multiplicity-one, Mathieu $M_{24}$ centraliser cells, $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}/24$ CICY scope, K3 Yangian abelian-at-Lie vs non-abelian-at-Hopf, $(24,24)$ MO-geometric realisation, heterotic matter vs Yangian Sugawara, $P_2(D)=0$ per-$D$ vs uniform, Miki $S_3$-triality, Dunn--Lurie real-vs-complex dim slip, CY$_3$-constraint forcing of $3^N$ denominators, $\mathbb Q$-span of $(J_2,J_3)$ regulators, cohomology $6^g$, CHL-only vs GC 8-form universal, chain-level $S^3$-framing toric-only, bare $\kappa$ subscript discipline, object-level vs $(\infty,1)$-functor Pattern 273, single-vs-three-source canonicity, Gaiotto $\Sigma_{0,24}$ genus-puncture.

Stage-$2$ specialisation of the $E_d$ Hochschild object over $\Sigma_{d-1}$ lands in $E_1$ on the reference curve for $d\ge3$. Primary: PTVV 2013 \emph{Publ IHES} 117 Thm 2.5; Calaque--Pantev--To\"en--Vaqui\'e--Vezzosi 2017; Costello--Gwilliam Vol II. | AP-CY263 / PTVV shift versus hCS BV-field shift |

Minimal model truncates: $\mu_n^{\min}=0$ for $n\ge 3$ by Bogomolov-Tian-Todorov unobstructedness of K3 plus Kunneth. | Block-diagonal $\At(T(K3\times E))=p_1^*\At(TK3)$; $\chi(\mathcal O_E)=0$ is Kunneth-shadow of $\At(TE)=0$, kills every mixed-tree integrand. ## 6d hCS audit + Harmonies synthesis append (2026-04-22, E1--E24 / AP-CY262--AP-CY285)

Twenty-four tip-table rows from the session's 6d hCS audit + Harmonies synthesis. Each row pairs with a working-cache entry E1--E24 in \texttt{notes/first\_principles\_cache\_comprehensive.md} and an AP-catalogue entry AP-CY203--AP-CY226 in \texttt{notes/antipatterns\_catalogue.md}; appendix numbered AP-CY262--AP-CY285 to avoid collision with the W11-19 parallel wave (AP-CY247--AP-CY261). Scope discipline: every claim about $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$, the seven framings, the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ tower, the dimensional siblings, and the six-route $G(K3\times E)$ construction must name its evidence class (proved, formal/open, conjectural) and its automorphic input (Siegel weight, paramodular weight, multiplier, level).

|---|-------------|---------------|---------------|---------------------|------|

## Wave-X cache append (2026-05-09 ChatGPT critique reconstitution)

Seventeen rows pinning the scope-omission collapses surfaced by the
May 2026 ChatGPT chiral-duality master critique
(`notes/chatgpt_chiral_duality_critique_consequence_map.md`) and
its deep adversarial review
(`notes/chatgpt_critique_consequence_map_adversarial_review.md`).
The deeper master pattern is **scope omission** along the three
orthogonal axes (level / chart / ambient); each row below pins the
healed form via the named comparison arrow under the named
hypotheses. AP-CY indices are the next-sequential block AP-CY455
through AP-CY471 (no parallel "Crit-N" numbering scheme).

| # | Wrong Claim | Ghost Theorem | Precise Error | Correct Relationship | Type |

| WX-1 | \(A\) is the primitive open object. | \(A_b=\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal C}(b)\) for a chosen boundary vacuum \(b\) is a useful chart-dependent description of the open structure. | Chart-vs-invariant collapse: the primitive is the open factorisation dg-category on \((X,D,\tau)\) with closed-colour input; \(A_b\) depends on the choice of \(b\). | Primitive package \((X,D,\tau;\mathcal C^{\mathrm{op}},b,A_b,Z^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathcal C),\Theta_{\mathcal C},\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathcal C})\); \(A_b\) enters only after \(b\) is named. | primitive/chart (AP-CY455) |

Modular functor on the open category. | open-vs-closed adjective (AP-CY459) |
